All Lena wanted in that moment was to kiss Kara’s forehead and to hold her face in her hands. Instead of doing something so extroverted, Lena placed her hand on Kara’s and squeezed. With her free hand, Lena grabbed her phone and opened the dial-pad.

 

“I get that.” Lena said, adding, “I guess I’ll just cancel our reservations at that hot-pot place you’ve been wanting to go to.”

 

Kara’s head snapped up. Suddenly, all the life had returned to Kara’s body and the woman almost flew out of her chair.

 

“You booked it?” Kara asked, almost yelling.

 

This reaction was worth the strings Lena had to pull to get ahead of the waiting list and make a reservation at this ludicrously exclusive restaurant.

 

“Was it supposed to be hard?” Lena asked, “But since you’re so tired, I guess I should just—“

 

Kara cut her off by jumping to her feet and saying, “Absolutely not, grab your coat. I’m driving us there.”

 

The Kryptonian was tapping away at her phone when Lena jumped to her feet. She could feel her heart lodge itself in her throat at the thought of being on that motorcycle. Or, god forbid, having to wrap her arms around Kara’s waist for an extended period of time.

 

Lena quickly said, “Santiago is waiting downstairs so—“

 

Kara cut her off, saying, “Not anymore, he’s not, I just… sent him home.”

 

The sound of a message being sent filled the room.

 

Lena blinked and said, “You what?”

 

Since when did her driver answer to Kara? What was she paying the man for if he was just going to so whatever he pleased and listen to someone other than his employer?

 

Kara must have noticed the look on her face because she said, “Don’t start picking on Santiago, I told him you were OK with it.”

 

“Which I am not!” Lena argued.

 

The two were standing by the front door now, face to face. Kara sighed and pocketed her keys before holding Lena by her arms.

 

Gently, Kara said, “Look, despite me not liking the name, I am now officially a superhero. So that means it’s basically my birthday today and I demand that you ride on my bike for my birthday.”

 

Lena guffawed, saying, “I’d rather walk there than get on that thing.”

 

The pout was back and in full-force. Kara frowned down at Lena, saying, “You gotta trust me, Lena. I’d never let anything happen to you.”

 

She could have said no. Lena could have called Santiago and have him come pick them both up and that would be the end of the story. But she had always been weak.

 

Lena groaned and grabbed her coat, ignoring Kara’s cheers of victory. Was it truly a victory if it was earned through dirty tricks? Lena didn’t think so.

 

“I should honestly ban you from pouting, you look pathetic.” Lena grumbled, adding, “I’m only doing this because you’ve had a big day.”

 

That’s the excuse she was using. Because it was easier than admitting she’d do anything to make Kara happy, including riding on a death machine.

 

As Kara slipped on her leather jacket, she said, “Yeah, yeah, you know you love me.”

 

It had come out so easily but Kara froze the moment she realised what she said. Lena’s heart leapt into her throat and began racing. Did she love Kara? Was this connection between them, this devotion on Lena’s part, love? Was love what made Lena bite her tongue when Alex threatened her? Was it love that made Lena stay up late for weeks to work on a supersuit for Kara?

 

Lena was afraid of the answer so she decided to play it off, rather than address it seriously. She could agonise over these questions another time.

 

With a coy smile, Lena asked, “Do I?”

 

“I-I didn’t mean to… I just,” Kara began to stammer out but Lena cut her off.

 

“Less talking, more walking, Kara.”

 

The two made their way downstairs to the parking lot. Kara’s bike was parked out front and Lena was reminded how large and heavy this mental contraption actually was. It was scary, damn it. She had never ridden on a bike and the thought of having no walls to protect her from incoming vehicles scared her shitless.

 

It was bloody cold, too. Even with Lena’s fluffy coat, her teeth were chattering and she was shivering. And how was Lena supposed to sit on this thing without flashing all of National City?

 

“Your helmet, m’lady.” Kara said, holding out a large helmet.

 

Lena was not ready for how heavy the helmet actually was and nearly dropped it. She was supposed to wear this thing on her head?

 

“This is going to ruin my hair and makeup.” Lena grumbled and frowned at Kara.

 

Kara smiled down fondly at her, saying, “I’d rather mess up your hair than risk you getting injured. I’m the invincible one here, not you.”

 

Lena huffed and crossed her arms over her chest. She knew she was being a brat about this but between the wind and the anxiety, she couldn’t bring herself to care. Kara was the one who insisted they ride on this damn thing anyway.

 

Kara sighed before rummaging around in the storage compartment. After a moment, she handed Lena a different helmet that didn’t cover the face.

 

“Here, wear this one. Your hair might get a bit tousled but your makeup should be fine.” Kara said, smiling so brightly at her.

 

For the first time in months, Lena pouted. She knew when she was defeated and she didn’t like the feeling. Still, she enjoyed Kara putting the helmet on for her.

 

“Why do you even wear a helmet?” Lena asked as Kara began getting ready.

 

“Gotta follow the law, Miss Luthor.” Kara said with a cheeky smile, “I am nothing if not a law-abiding citizen.”

 

Lena rolled her eyes but couldn’t fight off the smile that grew on her face.

 

Kara swung her leg over the bike and got the engine started. The loud noise made Lena jump which Kara (thankfully) didn’t notice. Instead, Kara looked back at Lena and patted the seat behind her. Lena steeled her nerves and marched forward.

 

It was more than a bit awkward trying to manoeuvre with high heels and a dress but she eventually managed to get comfortable. Lena was sure she looked ridiculous in this helmet that was probably ruining her hair right now.

 

The only thing left was to—

 

“Hold on tight.” Kara said from inside her helmet.

 

Lena immediately wrapped her arms around Kara’s waist and buried her face into Kara’s shoulder blades. She was more terrified of falling off this bike than she was embarrassing herself in front of her crush.

 

(It should be noted that Kara’s waist felt even smaller than Lena expected it to and she could feel Kara’s abs straining against her shirt. This is highly relevant information.)

 

When Kara was sure that Lena was secured, she patted Lena’s arms to comfort her before revving the engine and taking off into the night.

 

Now, Lena could have sworn that it was a twenty minute drive from Kara’s apartment to the restaurant. So the fact that Kara got them there in six minutes was both terrifying and slightly attractive. Lena had barely any time to enjoy the ride — even though she refused to open her eyes for most of it, anyway.

 

Kara pulled into the parking lot and Lena was almost sad the ride had ended. Despite the terrifying experience of riding shotgun on a motorcycle, being that close to Kara had been a highly enjoyable experience.

 

When Kara finished parking, she set down the kickstand and quickly dismounted. Before Lena could react, Kara was helping her stand without ruining her dress or hurting herself. It was… sweet, to say the least.

 

Kara took off her helmet and Lena was both jealous and dazzled by how good Kara’s helmet hair was. It was just the right amount of tousled to give her that rebellious look. Meanwhile, Lena was sure she looked like a harassed racoon under her helmet.

 

Grinning, Kara said, “We’ve reached our destination, Miss Luthor. How would you rate your experience?”

 

Lena straightened out her clothes and said, “It wasn’t terrible.”

 

“I knew you’d like it.” Kara said, bouncing on the balls of her feet.

 

“Let’s not push it. My hair’s still messed up and my dress is creased.” Lena said, ignoring how petulant she sounded.

 

She’d feel much better after she got something to eat. Kara stepped forward and unclipped the helmet on Lena’s head before gently pulling it off.

 

“I think you look great. Well, maybe not your hair.” Kara said, still smiling.

 

Lena was about to reach up and fix her hair when she felt Kara’s fingers already combing through her hair. Lena could do nothing except stare at Kara’s face while her cheeks burned and her felt shivers run down her spine.

 

Be still, my beating heart, Lena silently prayed. Kara had no idea what her mere touch could do to Lena and that was the worst part.

 

“There.” Kara murmured, “Perfect.”

 

As Kara pulled Lena into the restaurant, one thing became clear: this crush was beginning to blossom into something much bigger, and it had taken root inside her heart.

3. iii.

Summary for the Chapter:

this whole chapter can be summed up by that one tumblr post:

lena, crying: is it bc i'm a luthor?
kara, with angry tears: is it bc she's a luthor?

Shit was bound to hit the fan eventually. Lena just didn’t think it would happen so soon.

 

It had been a quiet evening for Lena. Normal workday followed by a trip to Kara’s apartment to unwind for the day. The two had been watching something on the television, Lena couldn’t even remember what it was, after eating dinner.

 

Lena had made her signature lasagna today in celebration. The two of them were laying on Kara’s pull-out sofa. Lena was sitting up against the sofa while Kara was sprawled out in the bed, twisted up in the blanket.

 

“Someone’s had a big week.” Lena said, adding, “First with that bank robbery then a burning building—”

 

Kara cut her off, saying, “Don’t forget the CatCo interview.”

 

Yes, Kara had caved in and gave an exclusive interview to Cat Grant. They hadn’t spoken much about it, considering Lena found out about it from the news.

 

“Isn’t that coming out tomorrow?” Lena asked, “A CatCo exclusive interview, they called it.”

 

Kara smiled sheepishly and said, “3PM sharp.”

 

Lena would clear her schedule if it meant not missing that interview. National City had been buzzing with discussion about the new superhero: it wasn’t every day that the cousin of Superman named themselves the defender of a lesser city. Some people thought that Supergirl would really put the city on the map, along with the likes of Metropolis and Gotham. Others weren’t as excited, fearing that a superhero would attract equally dangerous enemies.

 

Most people were just excited to have a superhero flying around.

 

“Well that’s still hours away. For now, we get to celebrate. You’re more of a white wine kinda girl, aren’t you?” Lena asked, getting up to grab the alcohol.

 

She absolutely wasn’t looking for an excuse to distance herself because Kara’s shirt had ridden up and her stomach was peeking out. Definitely not.

 

Kara, completely unaware, shrugged and said, “It just tastes better than red.”

 

Lena rolled her eyes and said, “Agree to disagree.”

 

Soon, the two were sitting together at the kitchen counter on the stools lined up there. Kara sat facing the door while Lena sat in front of her. Both were dressed in sweatpants because Kara had leant Lena her NCU hoodie to replace Lena’s pantsuit. The smell was distinctly Kara and it relaxed Lena far more than it should have.

 

At least Kara said she looked cute in her hoodie.

 

“How’s the DEO?” Lena asked, handing her the wine glass.

 

“Surprisingly, good. Well, it’s better than I thought it would. Hank still grills me on literally everything — I can’t catch a break with that guy. But the things they make me do feel… right , y’know? Like I’m being sent to the right places for the right reasons. And it helps that I get to work with Alex.” Kara said, smiling from ear to ear.

 

Lena still didn’t agree with working with the government in any capacity. However, the DEO did no harm so far and working with them made Kara happy so she’d let it go. For now.

 

Carefully, Lena asked, “How’s your sister? Haven’t seen her in a while.”

 

Which was true! Since Alex basically threatened Lena with imprisonment, Lena hadn’t seen the woman. Probably for the best really — Lena feared that the next time they met, the same amount of conflict would crop up.

 

“She’s great. After that dumb fight—“ Kara said but was cut off.

 

“Which you started.” Lena said and pointed a finger at her.

 

“Yes, yes, which I started,” Kara said with a huff, “we’ve been good. She still kicks my ass in training which kinda sucks but at least I’m learning how to fight properly.”

 

“I’m glad this is going so well for you.” Lena said, resting her hand on Kara’s knee.

 

“Me, too. I…” Kara trailed off, staring at the door.

 

Lena was so confused as she watched her friend seemingly stare at nothing. She looked back to follow Kara’s line of sight and found nothing except the front door. She turned back, ready to ask Kara if someone was here, but Kara suddenly stood up.

 

“Wait here.” Kara said, her face impossibly stern and still watching the door.

 

Lena called after her, saying, “Kara?”

 

Kara didn’t pause. She marched right to the front door and yanked it open. Lena’s heart leapt into her throat when she realised who was standing there.

 

Alex and Clark Kent.

 

“What’s going on?” Kara asked, her voice even.

 

Alex looked from Kara to Lena, seemingly in surprise. She almost looked guilty when she said, “We need to talk.”

 

“About what?” Kara asked before yelling, “Hey!”

 

Clark had marched straight past Kara into the apartment. Lena nearly fainted from fear when she realised he was advancing towards her, looking like an angry bull charging. Before Lena could even blink, the scene in front of her changed. Kara was suddenly standing in front of Clark and had his shirt balled up in her fist, blocking his path to Lena. The two of them glared at each other until Kara shoved him back, making him stumble slightly.

 

Kara was protecting her and while this should have made Lena feel loved, she was too scared to concentrate on anything else except the primal desire to run and hide.

 

Clark snarled, “You invited her into your house?”

 

Emboldened by Kara’s protection, Lena snarled back, “ She has a name.”

 

“Oh, we all know your name, Luthor.” He said before turning his attention to Kara, “Have you lost your mind?”

 

Kara ignored him and said, “Alex, you better start explaining and you better do it fast.”

 

Alex stepped forward and raised her hands, saying, “Everyone just calm down.”

 

She looked pointedly at Clark who grimaced but walked away eventually. Kara didn’t relax at this distance; if anything, she reached back and patted Lena’s knee as reassurance. Whether this was Kara’s sake or Lena’s or even both, Lena didn’t know. All she knew was that she was protected now.

 

Alex stepped towards Kara, saying, “We wanted to do this privately, Kara.”

 

In all honesty, Lena believed her. She doubted Kara’s sister wanted to cause a fight intentionally and they just hadn’t been expecting Lena to be at Kara’s apartment.

 

That did nothing to diffuse the current situation, however.

 

Kara said, “Whatever you have to say to me, you can say it in front of Lena.”

 

Diffuse the situation. That was Lena’s new goal. It was be in the interest of everyone if two Kryptonians didn’t level National City because of her presence.

 

Lena jumped to her feet and placed her hand on the small of Kara’s back. Kara turned to look down at her, her previous glower gone and replaced with a soft, concerned expression. Directly solely at Lena.

 

“Kara, maybe I should just go.” Lena said gently.

 

But Kara immediately frowned and said, “No, you aren’t the uninvited party here. You haven’t picked a fight with anyone.”

 

The implication hung heavy in the air. Lena glanced up at the offending parties, to find Clark glaring at her and Alex avoiding eye contact.

 

Alex was the one to break the silence, saying, “We’re worried about you, Kara.”

 

Kara scoffed, asking, “Why? Because I’m a superhero now? Because for the first time since I landed on this planet I’m not hiding anymore?”

 

“Kara, you know that I’m fine with you being Supergirl,” Alex said, “It’s just—“

 

Clark cut her off, asking, “Do you see no problem with buddying-up with a Luthor? Rao , of all the people in this city—“

 

It was Clark’s turn to be cut off when Kara said, “Lena has done nothing to make me doubt her intentions. She has been nothing but kind to me. She—“

 

“So what? You’re going to wait till you actually feel the knife in your back to realise you’re being used?” Clark asked, barely able to contain his anger.

 

Kara didn’t speak for a moment. Lena couldn’t see her face but she knew that Kara had been shocked into silence.

 

“You can’t be serious.” Kara breathed out, looking between her sister and cousin.

 

She settled on Alex, saying, “And you? You agree with him?”

 

“Kara—“ Alex began to say as she stepped forward to Kara.

 

It wasn’t a no. Alex hadn’t said no. That seemed to be the last straw for Kara, who blew up in a way that Lena had never seen before.

 

“Enough! Both of you!” Kara roared.

 

The apartment grew incredibly quiet. Lena could only hear Kara’s heavy breathing and her own heart beating in her ears. She was so grateful that Kara’s anger was directed away from her, in defence of her.

 

Kara glared at the two of them, saying, “Neither of you have any right to speak of trust.”

 

Alex practically flinched at her words and Lena almost felt bad. Clark just looked confused, his previous anger slowly dissolving as he realised what direction the conversation was going in.

 

Clark stepped forward and said, “Kara, just listen —“

 

Kara cut him off, snarling, “I’ve heard enough, Kal! It’s time for you to listen!”

 

He didn’t say a word.

 

“You barge into my house, uninvited. You insult me, you insult my guest! You preach to me about trust and call me I’m an idiot for trusting someone just because of who they’re related to!” Kara said, the venom dripping from her voice.

 

The two of them looked properly chastised but Lena couldn’t even feel satisfied about it: she never wanted Kara to fight with her family, especially not like this.

 

“You want to talk about trust, Kal? Let’s talk.” Kara said, “You were a grown man when I landed on Earth. You’re the only family I have left in this entire universe and what did you do? You abandoned me. You left me with a human family, with aliens who were strangers to me!”

 

Clark wasn’t looking at Kara anymore. He was looking down at his hands, even as Kara advanced towards him.

 

Kara continued, saying, “I never asked you for anything! Just five minutes of your time would’ve been enough. But you couldn’t even pick up the phone to check up on me. We have spoken all of five times since I landed here! Half of those times lasted less than a minute! And you want to talk to me about trust. How can I trust you after you abandoned me?”

 

Lena wanted to reach out and stop Kara, to tell her enough was enough. But she also knew that Kara was voicing pain that had festered for years and she had no right to interfere. So she listened. That was all she could do, really.

 

Kara was breathing hard at this point. She stood in front of Clark now, saying in a low voice, “As far as I’m concerned, Lena has treated me way better than you ever have. You don’t have the right to barge in and out my life whenever you please. Now leave.”

 

She walked away from him. She turned her back on Clark and Lena thought that would be the end of things. She thought the two of them would turn tail and run, until they made up again. Alex couldn’t look Kara in the eyes and Clark looked like he was about to cry.

 

Lena had been wrong. It seemed that stubbornness ran in Kara’s family, biological or otherwise.

 

“Kara,” Clark said, looking truly ashamed, “I’m sorry—”

 

Kara turned back and roared, “ Awuhkh ! Rrosh !”

 

Lena will never forget how her bones shook when she heard Kara speak in Kryptonian like that. Kara never spoke in her native tongue and Lena had never asked her to, understanding how sacred the language must be to Kara. But this? Hearing this felt like Lena was standing in the middle of a terrible storm, shaken to the core and hyperaware of how fragile she was.

 

Kara held so much rage and pain and they burst forth through those two words.

 

Clark, to his credit, didn’t seem scared. Not like Lena whose legs had turned to jelly. He gave Kara a teary-eyed look and left. His heavy footsteps seemed to echo through the apartment, in tandem like Lena’s beating heart.

 

The three women were left. Alex looked absolutely devastated that the confrontation had led to this. For once, Lena had no idea how Kara would react to her sister.

 

Alex stepped forward and lifted her hand, trying to hold Kara’s hand. Kara silently moved out of her sister’s grasp and took a step back. Alex looked like someone had slapped her.

 

Not looking at her sister, Kara murmured, “Just go, Alex.”

 

There was no malice in her voice. She just sounded defeated, as if all the energy had been sucked out of her.

 

At first, Alex didn’t move. She seemed frozen in place as a battle raged on inside her. Then, wordlessly, Alex left and shut the apartment door behind her.

 

Lena felt like she could breathe again. However, she immediately felt guilty for being relieved when that fight had affected Kara so badly. How dare she feel relief when Kara was grieving?

 

Speaking of Kara, she had yet to say anything. She stood in front of Lena with her back still turned to her. Lena couldn’t see anything except the shallow rise and fall of Kara’s shoulders.

 

Kara walked towards the pull-out bed and sat down, not sparing Lena a glance. She buried her head into her hands and soon, her shoulders began to shake. Soft sobs escaped Kara’s mouth and Lena found herself moving towards her, unthinking.

 

Lena sat down on the bed next to Kara. The poor woman was shaking like a leaf and struggling to get a hold of herself. She was unrecognisable from the woman who made Superman run away crying.

 

This wasn’t Supergirl right now. This was Kara Zor-El, and she needed someone to take care of her.

 

Lena rubbed Kara’s back, feeling the bones and muscles under her hand. Kara’s back shook with each sob that escaped. Kara still wouldn’t look at Lena, still hiding her head in her hands. She looked impossibly small right now and it killed Lena to see her like this.

 

This was the same Kara who would hold up traffic to help a family of ducks get across the street. The same Kara who brought Lena lunch whenever she thought she wasn’t eating enough. The same Kara who defended Lena time and time again, against the world at times.

 

She had to do something to comfort Kara. If not, Lena would never be able to live with herself.

 

Lena swallowed down the lump in her throat, saying, “Kara, look at me.”

 

Kara shook her head, still hiding. She whined pitifully as a response and Lena’s heart tore in two. She moved closer to Kara and wrapped her arms around her, making Kara sob even harder. Lena could now feel how the sobs wracked Kara’s body and how laboured each of her breaths were.

 

“I blew up again.” Kara cried.

 

Kara dipped her body down until her head and hands were sat in Lena’s lap. Lena blinked back tears as she rested her chin on Kara’s back, still rubbing circles there.

 

Lena said, “You had the right to. Kara, you did nothing wrong.”

 

Kara shook her head and said, “I didn’t want to start a fight with either of them. But they just barged in and started shouting and—“

 

Lena cut her off, saying, “Hey, hey, shush. It’s alright. I understand.”

 

Kara just cried harder. Lena didn’t know how to help Kara. She was terrible at processing and dealing with her own emotions, let alone comforting someone else. All Lena could do was hold Kara until the sobs died down and her tears ran dry. Until Lena’s hand had pins and needles from rubbing Kara’s t-shirt and her back grew stiff from curling around Kara’s body.

 

Lena never wanted any of this. All she had wanted was a friend. Now, thanks to the crimes of her family, Lena was tearing apart her friend’s family because of her mere presence in Kara’s life. Lena wondered if Kara would’ve been better off never meeting Lena — this fight never would’ve happened in that case.

 

But Lena was weak and selfish and she couldn’t imagine never meeting Kara. She didn’t want to. Kara was there for Lena when she needed it most, the least she could do was be here for Kara now.

 

There was always the chance that Kara decided Lena wasn’t worth the effort one day. There was the chance that she chose peace in her family over her friendship with Lena. As heartbreaking as that would be, Lena couldn’t control that.

 

What she could control was her transparency with Kara. If Kara was going to trust her, Lena needed to be honest with her. It’s all she could do to keep Kara by her side.

 

“I want you to hear this from me first.” Lena murmured after a shaky breath, “Your cousin does have a good reason not to trust me.”

 

Kara pulled out of Lena’s embrace just to shake her head. Her cheeks were bright red and streaked with tears, paired with a messy mane of blonde hair. She just looked so damn fragile that Lena couldn’t stop herself from fixing Kara’s hair and wiping her tears.

 

“Shush, just listen,” Lena said, still settling Kara’s hair.

 

Maybe Lena’s confession would drive Kara away. If so, she was going to savour this moment. She was going to memorise every part of Kara right now, from the scar near her eyebrow and the exact shade of blue in her eyes.

 

Finally, Lena dropped her hands and took a deep breath.

 

“Before Lex was arrested, we worked on a project together. He called it non nocere .” Lena confessed, searching Kara’s face for any reaction.

 

Kara nodded and said, “Do no harm. Latin.”

 

There was that big brain of hers. Kryptonians were natural polyglots, after all.

 

Lena smiled and said, “Clever girl. That was the premise of the project. We wanted to create a device that could influence people, make them more docile and agreeable.”

 

Kara leaned away and looked taken aback. Lena had almost forgotten how crippling fear could be as it rushed through her body, like acid being poured through her veins.

 

“Lena…” Kara breathed, a tinge of disappointment in her voice.

 

Lena looked away, saying, “I only wanted to use it on the extreme outliers in society. Aggressive prisoners, for example. I thought that if their anger was a learned behaviour, maybe we could fix them. Make them peaceful.”

 

The goals of a much younger, much more idealistic Lena. One that she tried to forget every single day.

 

“But playing god never works out well,” Lena said, “we tested the device on a group of prisoner inmates. Highly aggressive males convicted for violent crimes, all life sentences. I thought that if the device could fix them then it could fix anyone.”

 

Kara wasn’t leaning away anymore and she wasn’t frowning either. Lena felt like she could breathe again, even if she wasn’t out of the woods just yet.

 

She continued, saying, “The device made things worse, actually. We tried suppressing their desire to do no harm but that was in direct conflict with their survival instincts, their need to protect themselves. Our hubris caused a prison riot.”

 

“I know now that human emotion can’t — shouldn’t — be suppressed. It’s part of who we are. I wanted to just leave it at that.” Lena said, glancing at her hands.

 

She didn’t know if she wanted to continue. The bulk of the confession was done, there was no need to tell Kara about what came next. But wouldn’t a lie by omission still be a lie?

 

A warm hand curled around Lena’s. She nearly flinched at the sudden feeling and when she looked up, Kara was looking at her.

 

“But?” Kara asked, tilting her head.

 

Lena swallowed.

 

“But Lex had other ideas.” She said, “He knew the project was doomed to fail. He said he wanted me to see with my own eyes the true nature of humanity. That our desires and instincts are base, depraved. He said we needed someone to keep us in line, a leader.”

 

Kara’s hand was still in her own and Lena tried to draw strength from it. Quietly, she said, “A Luthor.”

 

A squeeze. Kara was still looking at her, searching for something in Lena as she spoke. Lena hoped she found whatever she was looking for. She wished that Kara could feel everything Lena was feeling through their linked hands.

 

Lena ran her free hand through her hair as she continued, saying, “We fought. When I realised how far gone Lex was, I was scared. So I had turned to the government to testify against him. To let someone know that Lex was losing his mind and to prepare for whatever he had planned.”

 

A pause. Lena was sure she looked particularly pathetic as she waited for Kara to understand what exactly she was referring to. It didn’t take long for Kara’s eyes to grow wide.

 

“The DEO.” Kara breathed out.

 

Lena nodded and smiled mirthlessly, saying, “Those bumbling idiots made everything worse. Of course Lex was going to notice DEO agents constantly shadowing him. They just made him more paranoid, pushed him to act faster than he might have otherwise.”

 

“Or maybe he would’ve done it anyway.” Kara argued, scrunching up her eyebrows.

 

“Maybe. Thanks to the DEO, I’ll never know.” Lena said, looking away.

 

Deep down, Lena knew that Lex was always going to go down this path. Superman had just been the catalyst, the excuse. When Lex viewed himself as the key to humanity’s salvation, there would always be an opportunity to create an enemy. If not Superman, then something else. Lex’s insanity had nothing to do with Superman and everything to do with Lex.

 

That didn’t mean it didn’t hurt.

 

“So, now you know.” Lena said, “I tried creating a mind control device to strip people of their free will. My darkest secret.”

 

It felt terrible, voicing this out loud. She had safely tucked away this part of her life into a box buried in her mind. Dredging it up like this was dangerous.

 

Lena looked at Kara and said, “Your cousin was right about me, you know. You can’t trust me.”

 

Kara shook her head furiously and covered their joined hands with her free hand, engulfing them. She asked, “Why? Because you made a mistake?”

 

Lena found herself laughing but it was sardonic at best. There was that halo again, glowing brightly from where it sat curled around Kara’s head. Was there no wrong that Lena could do in her eyes?

 

“We all make mistakes, Lena. You were trying to do something good. It’s not a sin to want a better world. What matters is what we do after we make mistakes.” Kara said, still searching for something in Lena’s eyes.

 

The tears pricked her eyes and Lena looked to the side, clenching her jaw. Crying was weak, damn it. Her mother would’ve punished her severely for daring to cry over something like this. Lex never would have cried.

 

Warm hands cupped Lena’s cheeks. She turned and found Kara holding her face, looking determined. Kara’s thumbs were wiping away the tears that spilled down Lena’s cheeks now. She hadn’t even realised she had started crying. Now that the flood gates had opened up, Lena couldn’t stop them.

 

Kara said, “Kal is wrong. You might be a Luthor but… but you are nothing like your brother. You’re the best example of what a Luthor could be.”

 

Lena wanted to hit her. She wanted to hit Kara over and over again until she understood that Lena was flawed, that she wasn’t infallible. Lena’s words had no affect so maybe her fists had a chance. Lena did not deserve this kind of unwavering loyalty, this absolute devotion. She had done nothing in her life to earn it.

 

And yet here Kara was.

 

It was Lena’s turn to be held now. It was her turn to be engulfed in strong arms and rocked as she wept. It was her turn to hold onto Kara, weak and shaking.

 

As Lena cried, she felt Kara shift against her. Kara had bent her head down and kissed the crown of Lena’s head, one hand still tangled in Lena’s hair.

 

Softly, Kara murmured, “I’ll do the surgery.”

 

Lena’s heart stopped in her chest.

 

She immediately pulled away and shook her head, saying, “Kara…”

 

Lena didn’t want Kara to make her decision like this. This felt wrong, it felt cheap. Kara just had a fight with her family and Lena had cried in front of her — it must have pushed her to say yes. Kara must’ve felt burdened, guilty, after everything that just happened. Kara was supposed to make a decision of her own free will, not influenced by Lena or anyone else. This wasn’t what she wanted.

 

“I trust you, Lena. No matter what anyone else says, I trust you. And I want to prove that.” Kara said, “Being powerless and trapped in a room for so long is kind of terrifying but… I know you’ll keep me safe.”

 

Trust. It was a word that held so much weight. For the longest time, Lena had worried that she couldn’t trust anyone. People were only in Lena’s life temporarily or because they had to be. Coworkers, business partners, employees, associates — no one stayed long or nobody wanted to. Lena had understood this. She put a wall between herself and the rest of the world, understanding that love and trust and happiness wasn’t for everyone. It certainly wasn’t for a Luthor.

 

Especially not her.

 

Until now. Now, Lena had someone who would move mountains for her. Someone who would defend her against the world, loved ones included. Someone who wanted nothing from Lena except friendship and gave everything she had to offer in exchange. Someone who snuck through the cracks in Lena’s walls with only the best of intentions.

 

For the longest time, Lena had worried that she couldn’t trust Kara. Not once did she consider that it was Kara’s trust she had to earn as well. Kara gave Lena loyalty and love and hope and wanted nothing in return.

 

Kara Zor-El was everything good in this universe. The least Lena could do was do right by her. She built that suit to protect Kara, just as Kara protected her tonight. She would use Kara’s skin sample to improve the lives of billions living on Earth, human or otherwise. Kara gave Lena so much and Lena needed to do something with what she was given so freely.

 

Kara had travelled the universe to be here in front of Lena, right now — Lena had to make that mean something.




later.




The operating room had been ready for use almost a week later. While Lena understood that Kara’s word, once given, was absolute, that didn’t mean she was no longer paranoid. Lena knew that this was a time-sensitive mission and the sooner she organised the surgery, the better her chances were that Kara still agreed.

 

Lena paid an inordinate amount of money to get the room ready. It wasn’t hard to get the bed and television installed, along with soundproof walls and a joint bathroom. No, the difficult part was selecting the surgeon and installing the red sun technology.

 

Thanks to Lena’s children’s hospital, she had access to a variety of surgeons and medical contacts. But of all the surgeons available, Lena needed to narrow down the ones who were both competent and discreet. There are plenty of news platforms that would pay good money to expose Lena Luthor for any shady dealings.

 

All it took was one scathing article about her to set L-Corp back once more.

 

Eventually, Lena settled on Yuta Nakamura, a fairly young surgeon who was making a name for himself among his peers. He was quite skilled at his craft and was young enough that he wouldn’t jeopardise his career to expose Lena.

 

He was the best available. Lena made him an offer and he had accepted. There was nothing more to say.

 

The other issue had been developing red sun lamps under such short notice. It took her a day to get the calculations right and the materials in order, and then another three days to actually get the lamps ready and working.

 

And Lena had seen to it personally that the room was livable. The last thing she wanted was to make Kara uncomfortable, especially given the circumstances.

 

Which is why she stood proudly in front of said room, smiling at Kara and fidgeting with her hands. Kara was wearing a white shirt and black sweatpants, as well as her usual boots. Lena had asked her to dress comfortable but discreetly.

 

Discretion was key in this situation.

 

Lena stopped Kara by placing her hand on Kara’s arm. Kara paused and looked down at Lena, curious. She didn’t look too anxious but Lena knew that anxiety could creep up suddenly and terribly.

 

Smiling, Lena said, “Thank you for doing this, Kara. I can’t thank you enough.”

 

Kara dipped her head and adjusted her glasses. Lena’s smile grew impossibly wider.

 

“Let’s get this over with.” Kara said before looking around, “This is where I’m staying?”

 

Lena nodded, saying, “Mhm. My team has already built in the red sun lamps along the ceiling. The absorption process is quite long so I hope you brought your homework and maybe a few games like I said to.”

 

There had been an extensive debriefing between the two friends which had mostly been Lena acting like a mother-hen and Kara nodding when appropriate.

 

Kara slipped her backpack off her shoulder, saying, “Actually, I already finished all my coursework. But! I did bring snacks and Animal Crossing so I should be good for a few hours.”

 

She pulled out a box filled with protein bars, sweets and fruits, along with this long, flat console that Lena didn’t recognise.

 

“I’m glad. Just ask and I can order food you want delivered.” Lena said, still watching Kara.

 

Kara cleared her throat, saying, “Sweet.”

 

She watched as Kara began to walk into the room, taking in surroundings drenched in red lighting. She plopped herself down on the bed and Lena immediately knew something was wrong. Kara began bouncing her leg and fiddling with her hands, eyes glancing around the room.

 

Lena walked towards her and her heels clicked against the floor. Kara made a point to look anywhere except at Lena.

 

She stood in front of the Kryptonian now. Kara was looking down, still bouncing her knee.

 

“Kara.” Lena murmured, “Look at me.”

 

Kara sighed and looked up at Lena. As gently as possible, Lena took Kara’s glasses off and folded them neatly before placing them on the table beside her. Her face always looked so bare and vulnerable without the glasses on, especially with the civilian clothes she wore.

 

“Are you nervous?” Lena asked, taking a seat next to Kara on the bed.

 

Kara hummed as she looked down at her hands, saying, “The last time I was stuck in a small space it didn’t end well.”

 

Lena’s throat closed up. Just thinking about Kara being trapped in that tiny pod for so many years was sickening. She could only imagine how Kara felt considering she had experienced it.

 

“Do you wanna talk about it?” Lena asked, tilting her head.

 

Kara shook her head, saying, “There’s not much to say. I’ve heard claustrophobia is quite common among humans, it’s not a big deal.”

 

Lena knew exactly what Kara was doing, sweeping her feelings under the rug and never looking at them again. After all, Lena did that every day. Rugs weren’t so different from little boxes, really.

 

“It’s not insignificant either, darling.” Lena said.

 

Kara didn’t respond. Lena left it at that: probing Kara right now would only make the woman clam up or worse, trigger something. Instead, Lena leaned against Kara’s shoulder and sighed when she felt Kara’s hand gripping her thigh.

 

“Will you come visit me? Just once before the surgery starts.” Kara asked, flashing her those puppy-dog eyes.

 

Lena paused before pulling away. Kara blinked owlishly at her. Did… Did Kara think…

 

“Oh, Kara, I’m not working today. I took the day off.” Lena said, shocked that Kara would ever think otherwise.

 

Kara was taken aback, saying, “What?”

 

Lena raised an eyebrow, asking, “Did you really think I’d just leave you by yourself in here? Give me some credit. We’ll be together until the surgery finishes.”

 

It didn’t take long for Kara to pull Lena into her arms, even in this awkward position. All Lena could think about was how warm Kara’s body felt against hers, the smell of toothpaste that lingered and how she suddenly couldn’t breathe.

 

Lena patted Kara’s back, wheezing out, “Kara, I can’t breathe.”

 

Kara immediately let go and assessed Lena for injuries. Despite herself, Lena found herself giggling at the Kryptonian. Kara gave her a small smile of her own and Lena counted that as a victory.

 

“Thank you.” Kara murmured.

 

It only got easier from there. The two friends relaxed and began filtering around the room, each doing their own thing. Lena had brought her work laptop to kill time by going through her to-do list. Kara was sprawled out on the bed, shoes kicked off and enchanted by whatever game she was playing. The television was playing softly in the background.

 

Often, Kara would decide to annoy Lena through various methods. Sometimes she’d place snacks on Lena’s table and stare at her until she acknowledged they were there. Lena decided she was a fan of the kona coffee mochi — the rest of the snacks weren’t worth mentioning. Other times, Kara would make Lena play games on her Switch console which lead to an argument.

 

(“This is horribly inefficient!” Lena said, “Why can’t you, as a free citizen of this island, create your own lumber business? I’ve seen plenty of shops, why can’t you use your bells to—“

 

“This game is meant for relaxation!” Kara argued, “You collect the wood, you fish the fish, you build the house — it’s about time management and organisational skill development!”

 

“It’s inefficient .” Lena seethed.

 

Kara yelled, “Maybe if you played the game correctly—“

 

“How dare —“)

 

All in all, Lena enjoyed herself. She didn’t get as much work done as she would’ve liked but it was difficult to balance work with a hyperactive golden retriever.

 

Eventually, Lena’s timer went off. It was officially three in the afternoon and Supergirl’s first ever interview was going to be broadcasted on their news station.

 

Kara’s head popped up at the sound and she looked at Lena curiously.

 

Lena held the television remote and asked, “Do you mind if we watch your interview?”

 

“Oh, I forgot about that.” Kara said, nodding.

 

“I certainly haven’t. It’s your first televised interview, Kara.” Lena chastised her, already flicking to the CatCo channel.

 

Kara said, “You sound like an excited parent.”

 

Lena looked back and found Kara smiling at her, curled up in a blanket burrito. Childish but adorable.

 

“I feel like one.” Lena said, smiling back.

 

She didn’t need to look at Kara to know the woman was sticking her tongue out at her. Instead, Lena turned to look at the television as the recorded interview began. It was audio-only and the news station had created a transcript that appeared on the screen when spoken.

 

The audio began and Lena sat forward on her seat.

 

“So, who are you exactly?” Cat Grant’s voice rang out.

 

“I’m Supergirl, apparently,” Kara’s voice answered, “isn’t that what you’ve named me?”

 

Lena raised an eyebrow at Supergirl’s tone. She had never heard Kara speak so sternly, like a ruler addressing her lessers. It was… attractive, among other things.

 

Cat said, “Trademark pending. Where are you from?”

 

“Krypton, my home planet in the—“ Kara was cut off.

 

“I’ve already heard this story before.” Cat said, feigning boredom, “Any affiliation with Superman?”

 

Lena was already growing tired of Cat’s behaviour. Perhaps she was just too protective of Kara.

 

“We’re cousins, actually.” Kara confessed.

 

“Oh. So convenient acts of heroism run in the family then?” Cat asked.

 

There was a pause and even Lena froze.

 

Finally, Kara asked, “Convenient?”

 

“Well, I’m sure you have your reasons for popping up now instead of two years ago during the earthquake, or those bushfires that killed eight people, or—“ Cat was cut off.

 

“It’s complicated. All you need to know is that I’m here now and I just want to help. I want to protect everyone living on Earth, humans and aliens alike.” Kara said, unable to stop the passion in her voice.

 

Help. Everyone could use a bit more help in the world. Who would turn down a helping hand when it was offered so freely?

 

Cat quickly changed the topic, asking, “Any comment on President Olivia Marsdin’s Alien Amnesty Act?”

 

Kara’s voice came out crystal-clear. With a hint of steel in her voice, she said, “Don’t try to involve me with human politics, Miss Grant.”

 

Behind her, Kara tapped Lena’s shoulder to get her attention.

 

“I actually support it.” Kara whispered loudly, adding, “Obviously.”

 

Lena smiled and turned back around. She expected nothing less. The president’s decision had been a hotly debated topic for months and it had recently come to fruition when the act was instituted. Now, all aliens could claim asylum in America and be granted amnesty.

 

Supergirl included.

 

“Fine. Have it your way.” Cat said with a bit of bite, “That suit — did you make that?”

 

There was a small chuckle before Kara said, “Actually, it was a gift.”

 

Lena’s heart began to race. She turned back to look at Kara who just grinned sheepishly at her.

 

“From?” Cat asked.

 

The world stood still for a few moments.

 

“Lena Luthor.”

 

Lena suddenly couldn’t breathe. Did that actually happen? Did Lena just fall asleep at this desk and was experiencing a fever dream right now? Did Supergirl actually mention Lena’s name on national television to millions of viewers? In a positive light?

 

The familiar whoosh of Kara’s cape could be heard, signalling the end of the interview.

 

Still, Cat’s voice called after her, asking, “What is your relationship with Lena Luthor? Do you have a day job?”

 

Lena might’ve laughed if she wasn’t shocked into silence. Her brain was still trying to process what had just happened.

 

Slowly, she turned back to look at Kara. The blonde woman was still smiling sheepishly and blushing a bit.

 

Lena asked, “You… you told everyone I made the suit for you?”

 

Kara blinked. Slowly, she asked, “Was I not supposed to?”

 

“No, no, it’s fine. I just… wasn’t expecting it, that’s all.” Lena said, still wrapping her head around the implications of that interview.

 

L-Corp’s board of directors were going to have a field day.

 

“Well, the public is just going to eat this up, aren’t they?” Lena said, grinning, “A Luthor and a Super, working together.”

 

If the blinding smile on Kara’s face was any indication, Kara seemed to like that idea.




later.




The sun had already dipped below the horizon by now. Both Lena and Kara were growing bored in their current situation, despite their attempts to alleviate it. They had ordered takeout — Lena ordered the vegetarian pesto pasta and Kara had ordered a host of dishes that Lena had already forgotten. After lunch, Kara had made Lena watch both the Lion King and Mulan and no matter what Kara said, Lena absolutely did not cry at any point during those three hours.

 

There was just dust in her eyes at specific points in the movie.

 

Anyway, it was close to eight o’clock now and the doctor should be arriving soon, if he hadn’t arrived already. Lena had instructed him to contact her secretary if there was any trouble accessing the building. So far, Jess hadn’t told her about anything like that.

 

At the current time, Lena was arguing with Kara about their differing diets and weighing the pros and cons of each. This was apparently how an alien and a genius killed time.

 

“All I’m saying is that you could definitely go vegetarian for a month. Just give it a try!” Lena said, sipping on her drink. She wasn’t used to straws this big, nor tapioca balls mixed into her drinks.

 

Kara shook her head, saying, “Absolutely not, I need meat to survive! If I don’t get ten kilograms of meat in my body per day I could die!”

 

“That is factually untrue and you know it.” Lena argued, adding, “All you need is a big ole’ bowl of vitamin D.”

 

Kara waved her off but Lena was too busy focusing on the drink Kara had ordered for her. What had she called it? Boba?

 

“Damn, this is nice.” Lena said after swallowing another big gulp.

 

The taste wasn’t too overpowering or sweet and the pearls at the bottom were a nice touch.

 

Kara grinned and said, “See! I told you, boba is amazing. Milk tea boba is the best.”

 

Three large cups of boba tea sat on the desk near Kara, all emptied long ago. They had all been different flavours but the one Lena was drinking, the milk tea, had been among them. God, the appetite this woman had. Lena thought that maybe her food intake would decrease but it seemed to have the opposite effect. Maybe Kara’s body was compensating for the red solar energy? Lena wasn’t sure.

 

Still, she had to investigate.

 

“So how are you feeling?” Lena asked, setting her cup down.

 

Kara scoffed, saying, “Not so super.”

 

Outwardly, nothing appeared amiss with Kara. She might’ve been a bit sluggish but Lena attributed that to the boredom. 

 

“Well, that’s great! Do you mind if we test something?” Lena asked, spinning around in her chair.

 

She pointed at Kara’s ‘pen’ that she had placed on the TV stand when she first came into the room. Lena said, “Pick that up for me.”

 

Kara rose to her feet and made her way over. Lena watched as Kara tried to pick the pen up like she always did, pinched between her thumb and index finger. The pen didn’t even budge and Kara took a step back in surprise. Once again, Kara grabbed the pen with her whole hand and braced herself. This time she actually picked it up but it was clearly taking a lot more effort to carry it.

 

“Rao, was this pen always so damn heavy?” Kara asked as she set it down.

 

Lena nodded, saying, “Always. And you usually pick it up like it weighs nothing.”

 

Kara pouted and said, “This sucks.”

 

She wished she didn’t find Kara whining about losing her super-strength cute. Instead of mentioning that, Lena stood up and made her way over to Kara.

 

Placing a hand on Kara’s arm, Lena said, “It’ll be over soon. I’m just waiting for the surgeon, Jess tells me he has yet to arrive.”

 

At that very moment, Lena felt her phone buzz in her pocket. She took it out and found a new message from Jess. The contents of the message made Lena frown, re-reading the text.

 

After a moment, Kara asked, “Is everything OK?”

 

Lena pocketed the phone: she’d have to deal with this herself. Kara would have to stay alone in this room for a short while. It pained Lena to leave her but she absolutely couldn’t let Kara be seen now. That commotion was bound to spell trouble.

 

“Give me a few minutes, dear. There’s apparently a commotion outside L-Corp.”

 

Lena squeezed Kara’s arm and the Kryptonian just nodded, still concerned. With one last look, Lena grabbed her blazer and left the room. She’d stop for nothing and no one until she had Kara back in her line of sight.

 

The ‘commotion’ outside L-Corp turned out to be a swarm of journalists and paparazzi alike. They were practically blocking the entrance and Lena was just grateful they weren’t here during work hours. Security had been keeping them in check but the moment they saw Lena walking towards them, they came to life.

 

Jess was waiting for Lena by the doors.

 

“The doctor is just around the corner, he thought it might be best not to come through the front doors.” Jess quickly said, glancing between Lena and the crowd.

 

Lena nodded and said, “He’s right. I’ll distract the journalists, lead him in through the side entrance, please.”

 

“Of course, Miss Luthor.” Jess said before rushing off.

 

Lena quickly set a five minute timer on her phone: that should be enough time for Jess to sneak the surgeon in. Taking a deep breath, Lena steeled her nerves before walking outside. The security made a space for her to stand and Lena felt bad that they had to hold back these rabid animals. Cameras were flashing and Lena could sense the migraine coming on

 

“Miss Luthor!” They called, “Lena Luthor!”

 

“Is it true that you built a supersuit for Supergirl?” One reporter yelled.

 

Another asked, “What’s your relationship to Supergirl?”

 

This one made Lena scoff which was the most they’d get out of her tonight.

 

“The public has a right to know the nature of your relationship, Miss Luthor!” A particularly entitled journalist yelled out.

 

Lena didn’t even acknowledge her: that would be exactly what she wanted. It was only when she felt the timer go off in her pocket that she told the journalists she wouldn’t be answering questions tonight and to go home. That’s all she said before heading back inside.

 

Sure enough, Jess and Doctor Nakamura were waiting inside for her. They were obscured from the journalists outside so, all things considered, the plan was going along smoothly.

 

“I apologise for that, doctor. Journalists tend to be insatiable when it comes to hounding my family.” Lena said, shaking his out-stretched hand.

 

He smiled and said, “That’s quite alright, Miss Luthor.”

 

Jess took her leave and Lena nodded at her, a small expression of gratitude. Turning back to the surgeon, Lena smiled back and said, “Please, call me Lena.”

 

“Right, Lena.” He spoke a little more seriously than before, saying, “Do you mind showing me where I’ll be operating?”

 

“Of course, follow me.” Lena said as they began to walk.

 

The walk from there to the operating room would be short so Lena decided to take the opportunity where she could.

 

“I do hate going through formalities, doctor, but they must be discussed.” Lena said, “Payment will be provided after the surgery is completed, as we agreed. Neither you or the patient will know each other’s names. This is strictly under the table.”

 

“You have my word, Miss Luthor.” Nakamura said with a gentle smile.

 

Neither of them could afford to rat out the other: that’s why Lena chose him.

 

They made it to the operating room with no hiccups. The room had been cleaned and Kara was pacing around the room when they arrived, making Lena’s chest clench painfully. She wished that she didn’t have to leave Kara for so long but it had been necessary. Still, the clear relief on Kara’s face when she spotted Kara was heartbreaking.

 

When they were all situated in the room, Lena said, “This is your surgeon. I’ve already discussed confidentiality with him.”

 

Kara just nodded and smiled tightly at the surgeon. He returned the smile. It was all very awkward, really.

 

“There’s no need to worry, miss. I’ve done plenty of surgeries far more complex than a simple skin biopsy.” Nakamura said.

 

Lena had never been so grateful to a stranger before as she watched Kara’s shoulders droop with relief.

 

“Thank you, doctor.” Kara said sincerely.

 

“Where exactly will the surgery be done?” Nakamura asked, adding, “On your body, I mean.”

 

Lena wanted to let Kara choose and she had told the surgeon exactly this.

 

Kara touched the space on her body, saying, “Here on my back, maybe near my ribs.”

 

The surgeon nodded and said, “Right. And, I have to ask, do you consent to this invasive surgery and have come here of your own free will?”

 

“I have.” Kara said.

 

“Please remove all clothing above the waist and lie on the bed, face down. The surgery should last no longer than twenty minutes from now, it’s quite a simple process, really.” Nakamura said, as easy-going as ever.

 

Kara nodded and turned around to face the wall. Lena flushed as she watched Kara pull her shirt off, revealing a well-defined back. She quickly faced away from Kara and turned her attention to Nakamura, who was also avoiding looking in that direction.

 

“Miss Luthor, a word.” He said, lowering his voice beside her.

 

Lena looked at him curiously.

 

“Has the patient taken any drugs or alcohol in the last 24 hours?” Nakamura asked.

 

She shook her head, saying, “None. She hasn’t been fasting but she doesn’t use substances of any kind. That includes pain medication.”

 

“Right.” He nodded before asking, “You sure she’ll be alright?”

 

Lena truly appreciated the concern. In her line of work, it was rare to see someone caring about the wellbeing of others. If only Lena’s passion had been medicine instead of science, maybe her life would’ve been better.

 

Regardless, Lena said, “She’s a tough one.”

 

Kara had been settled into bed with her back exposed and chest pressed against the mattress. Nakamura took his briefcase and placed it on the desk near the bed, beginning to go through its contents. Lena glanced at Kara and found her looking right at Lena.

 

Suddenly, Kara asked, “Excuse me, doctor?”

 

He turned back to look at Kara.

 

“Can Lena stay here?” She asked, sounding so small and nervous.

 

Lena’s heart swelled. The surgeon looked between the two of them, saying, “Given the circumstances, I’ll allow it. As long as Miss Luthor keeps a reasonable distance, of course.”

 

Kara sighed in relief. Lena rushed forward and sat on the stool in the corner of the room, the one directly across from Kara. Now the two of them were a metre apart, facing each other.

 

The operation began soon after that. Kara was still a bit anxious but Lena’s gaze seemed to calm her, it seemed. Lena understood the anxiety and wished there was a better way than this.

 

Nakamura said, “Alright, miss, I’m going to disinfect the area. I’ve been told that numbing cream won’t be necessary.”

 

Lena doubted it would’ve had any effect, considering Kara’s physiology and even with the red solar radiation. Kara jumped when she felt the cold, disinfectant-soaked cotton ball touch her skin. Since it was such a small area, this process was done quickly.

 

“There will be a stinging pain once I begin, feel free to stop me if the pain becomes too great and we can take a break until you’re ready to continue.” He said as he put on his sterile gloves.

 

The anxiety was back. Kara was looking at Lena for something, anything, to distract her from what was about to happen. She had put Kara in this situation and it was her responsibility to take care of her.

 

So Lena talked. She talked and talked until Kara wasn’t focused on the feeling of a blade slicing her skin. She talked about stocks and the economy and politics and what new brand of iced coffee she was trying out. And it seemed to work: Kara even cracked a smile at one point.

 

It was surreal to see Kara, this nigh-invincible being, bleeding.

 

Lena talked until the guilt stopped eating away at her and the surgery had finished. The surgeon applied the dressing to the incision site and Kara winced. The piece of skin that had been extracted had already been placed in a sterile container and all that was left was this dressing application.

 

Lena held two thumbs up to Kara who laughed breathlessly at the sight. Kara had handled the surgery beautifully, all things considered. And she had done it all for Lena.

 

“All done, miss. I’ve applied the dressing, I’m sure you’re aware of standard wound care. The incision was fairly shallow so it should heal fairly quickly.” Nakamura said as he put away his tools.

 

Kara sat up, saying, “Thanks, doc.”

 

Lena understood that on Krypton, women could be shirtless just as the men could and no one would bat an eye. But Lena was born on Earth and was incredibly attracted to women (Kara especially), so the glimpse of Kara’s exposed chest nearly made her fall off her seat.

 

When she was sure that Kara was dressed, Lena stood up and followed Nakamura out. After closing the door behind her, Lena took out the envelope from her blazer’s inner breast pocket and handed it to the surgeon. In return, the surgeon handed her the skin sample container.

 

“Thank you, doctor. Buy your daughters something nice for Christmas.” Lena said, smiling.

 

He laughed and said, “You got it, Miss Luthor.”

 

Lena didn’t bother walking him out: Jess was waiting to escort him, anyway. Instead, Lena walked back inside and took out her phone. Kara was standing up now, fully clothed and wearing her glasses. Lena used the app installed on her phone to turn the red sun lamps off. The room slowly lost its’ faint red hue, much to their relief: that meant they could both leave.

 

“How do you feel?” Lena asked, making her way over.

 

Kara smiled and said, “Better now that it’s over.”

 

A small part of Lena wanted to lift up Kara’s shirt to examine her dressing. The thought was quickly tossed from her mind as quickly as it appeared.

 

“You did great,” Lena said, adding, “I’d pat you on the back but…”

 

“Very funny.” Kara grumbled but couldn’t keep the smile off of her face.

 

The two fell into a short silence and Lena’s guilt caught up with her.

 

Gently, Lena said, “Hey, Kara.”

 

Kara tilted her head down to look at Lena.

 

“I… I just wanted to thank you again—“ Lena began saying but was cut off.

 

Kara said, “You gotta stop thanking me, Lena. Just put that sample to good use, yeah?”

 

Lena nodded quickly, saying, “Oh, I will. This will help so many people, Kara, I promise.”

 

The container in Lena’s pocket could very well be the key to fixing the global threat of excessive carbon emissions. It certainly wouldn’t go to waste, as long as Lena was alive.

 

“I know you will.” Kara said softly.

 

There it was again. That look of pure adoration on Kara’s face. The one that Lena had done nothing of substance to earn. Despite not earning it, Lena’s heart fluttered at the sight. She hoped that Kara never stopped looking at her like that.

 

Kara suddenly asked, “Can we please get something to eat now? I can only drink so much boba tea.”

 

Food. It was always food with this woman. Lena rolled her eyes and asked, “Noonan’s?”

 

“Noonan’s.” Kara agreed.

 

Lena had already arranged for this room to be cleared out by midnight tonight. For now, all Lena had to worry about was feeding a post-surgery, hungry Kryptonian.

 

Saving the world from ecological disaster seemed like an easier task now.




later.




Things had settled down after that. Kara was back to her Supergirl duties and Lena was balancing her work with her research. She had already made good progress with analysing the skin cells provided and was just working out logistics with her team before she approached the board.

 

The last thing Lena needed right now was to look foolish in front of those leeches.

 

Lena didn’t angst over the minimal contact with Kara lately: she was sure that Kara was swamped between college and being Supergirl. The few texts and calls here and there were enough for Lena. And Kara never failed to attend their Tuesday lunches, so Lena had no reason to worry.

 

So Lena was quite surprised when Jess informed her that she had a visitor. A certain journalist from the Daily Planet, in fact.

 

Turning him away never crossed Lena’s mind. No matter how angry Clark had gotten that day, Lena knew he would never actually hurt her. That was one line that he and Kara refused to cross — harming civilians. Besides, Lena had no reason to hide because she had done nothing wrong.

 

So there he stood, wearing those clunky glasses and oversized suit to hide the physique underneath. How he and Kara thought the glasses were the pinnacle of disguises was beyond Lena.

 

“What can I do for you, Clark?” Lena asked once he stopped in front of her desk.

 

Clark sighed and said, “I just wanna talk.”

 

Lena scoffed, saying, “We’ve talked enough, don’t you think?”

 

The two were staring at each other now, neither wavering or looking away. Lena refused to give him the satisfaction. Eventually, however, Clark had been the one to close his eyes and sigh. He seemed to be doing that a lot today.

 

“I remember when I first met you.” Clark said, his voice almost wistful.

 

Lena raised an eyebrow at this. She also remembered her first meeting with Clark. She had no idea why Lex, a genius in his own right, hung out with this awkward nerd from Kansas. He was so far from Lex’s usual circle of friends and honestly, that should’ve been the first red flag.

 

She should've known that there was more to Clark Kent than he let on.

 

Clark continued, saying, “Lex had bragged about you so many times that when I actually met you, I had no idea what to expect. You had accomplished way more than I had at 22. I was so intimidated.”

 

“Oh, how times change.” Lena said, smiling mirthlessly.

 

“You knew Lex better than anyone. Better than I did. Better than I thought I did, actually.” Clark said, “So you know that he was completely normal before…”

 

Lena could fill in the blanks as he trailed off. She asked, “Before he tried to kill you?”

 

“Before he lost his mind.” Clark corrected her.

 

Lena paused. Few people really knew Lex and even fewer saw his descent into madness. Most people thought that Lex just snapped one day and went on a rampage. In reality, the descent had been slow to start.

 

“I never saw his betrayal coming. I saw my friend who was ready to help me without a second thought. I saw the best of humanity in Lex.” Clark said.

 

It was strange. The way Clark talked about Lex sounded like how Kara talked about Lena. The comparison between their relationships made Lena’s skin crawl.

 

“I trusted Lex the same way Kara trusts you. Can you understand why that’s terrifying, Lena?” Clark asked.

 

Lena did understand. On a rational level, Lena understood exactly why past trauma made it difficult for people to trust again. But Lena was only human and hearing herself being compared to her murderous, lunatic brother didn’t sit right with her. She had fought her whole life to stand outside of Lex’s shadow, to make a name for herself. After everything that happened, Lena now fought against people who believed she turned out just like Lex.

 

Kara had assured Lena that she wasn’t like her brother so many times that Lena was beginning to believe it. Now here Clark Kent was, in his stupid spectacles and his stupid Southern drawl, telling Lena the exact opposite.

 

Of course she snapped. 

 

“I don’t owe you anything. You know nothing about me and you know nothing about Kara. Tell me, why have you suddenly decided to act like a protective big brother to Kara? Did your guilty conscience catch up with you?” Lena asked, a malicious smile stretched across her face.

 

Clark, to his credit, took her words in stride. He held her gaze as he said, “I have my own reasons for distancing myself from Kara.”

 

Lena laughed and asked, “Oh, yeah? Then you might try explaining that to her. Because right now you look like a paranoid stranger telling her who she can and can’t be friends with.”

 

She remembered how small Kara felt that night, curled up against Lena as she sobbed. All thanks to Clark’s brilliant plan to have a Luthor-centric intervention at her apartment, uninvited and unannounced. 

 

Clark squared his shoulders, saying, “The reason I came here today wasn’t to argue with you.”

 

“Seems like it.” Lena said, already growing tired of this conversation.

 

He stepped forward. On instinct, Lena leaned back into her chair away from him. She might be angry at him but Clark was still a powerful man and Lena was only human. Her only line of defence was the knowledge that Kara would never forgive Clark if he harmed Lena in any way — and he must know that as well.

 

Clark’s face softened and, for a moment, Lena saw the family resemblance. Those blue eyes were just as sad on Clark as they were on Kara.

 

“I wanted to apologise.” Clark said, “I can’t stand for truth, justice and the American way if I don’t follow due process. I can’t brand you guilty without proof — I shouldn’t.”

 

For once, he and Lena agreed. She remained silent.

 

He continued, saying, “But I also can’t look at you without seeing your brother.”

 

Lena sighed. She had expected that sentiment already.

 

“So where do we go from here, Clark?” She asked.

 

Clark stepped forward as he spoke, “So far, you haven’t hurt Kara. You might even care for her genuinely. I’ll leave it at that.”

 

“Just like that?” Lena asked, squinting her eyes at him.

 

“Just like that. Unless you give me reason to doubt your intentions.” Clark said, a hint of steel under that Kansas wheat of his.

 

There was nothing more to say, really. Silence filled the room and the two were left staring at each other. Lena tried to find pieces of Kara in him again but found nothing. Nothing except those heart-breaking blue eyes that House El seemed to possess.

 

Clark understood the silence for what it was. He turned to leave and Lena almost let him go. But one thought seized hold of her and she couldn’t stop herself from voicing it.

 

“Oh, before I forget.” Lena called after him.

 

He stopped by the doorway and turned back to look at her, curious.

 

“Congratulations. I heard the wife is pregnant.” Lena said.

 

Clark definitely hadn’t been expecting that. Quietly, he thanked her.

 

Before she lost her nerve, Lena asked, “Will the child even know about Kara?”

 

Lena hadn’t asked this to hurt him. She asked on behalf of Kara who had lost so much of her family already. It would devastate her if she wasn’t a part of Clark’s child’s life, even if she never voiced these thoughts openly. The least Lena could do was speak for her, when Kara was too kind to speak for herself.

 

Even if that meant earning the ire of the most dangerous being on the planet. At least she tried.

 

But Clark didn’t get angry. He looked away and clenched his jaw. There seemed to be a war raging inside him, over what Lena didn’t know. In the end, she wasn’t entitled to know. But his answer came anyway, as solemn as any prayer:

 

“I try not to make the same mistake twice, Lena.”




later.




Doctor Parker, the head scientist of Lena’s research team, was having a field day with the skin sample Lena gave him. He was like a kid in a candy store, really. He was hunched over the microscope as he spoke, saying, “The cell structure is remarkable. I’ve never seen anything quite like it.”

 

On that, Lena could agree. It was strange to think that such a strange, foreign cell was what made up Kara. This was a completely unknown, alien specimen they were studying — any scientist would be excited.

 

Looking up, Parker said, “You’re very lucky to have a friend like Supergirl. How’d you even get a skin sample from her? Doesn’t she have bulletproof skin?”

 

“Oh, she does.” Lena confirmed.

 

Parker blinked at her, clearly waiting for an explanation.

 

She rolled her eyes, saying, “I can’t give away all my secrets.”

 

Lena’s phone buzzed so she was distracted from Parker’s complaints. Her heart skipped a beat when she realised she had gotten a text from Kara, telling her that she’d better be going home because it was so late. In truth, Lena hadn’t even noticed that it was close to midnight now.

 

She stood up and said, “How about we wrap this up early? We can discuss revising our plans to replicate the cell. I was thinking maybe—”

 

Parker cut her off, saying, “Go, before you stay another hour.”

 

She had only done that twice, damn it, it wasn’t a thing with her. Lena grumbled to herself, saying, “Kicked out of my own lab. Tch .”

 

Lena knew that Parker only meant well, though. Workaholics like them tended to look after each other more than they looked after themselves. Lena often worked until she couldn’t see straight anymore, which was taking a toll on her health. A warm bath at home would do her wonders.

 

She collected the skin sample before leaving the lab. She had made it a habit to take it home with her, knowing how precious it was and how easy it would be to be lost or stolen. Lena couldn’t afford that. When it wasn’t being studied, it lived in Lena’s apartment where it was stored at optimal temperatures.

 

You couldn’t be too careful when trying to save the world.

 

L-Corp after dark had always scared Lena. While she had too much pride to ask security to escort her, Lena wasn’t above looking over her shoulder when she felt uneasy. Usually, it was nothing. She had accepted that she was a paranoid person and decided it was better to be safe than sorry.

 

But tonight, Lena looked over her shoulder and something had been there. Heavy, muffled footsteps were coming up behind her — two pairs by the sound of it. Lena could feel her heart hammering in her chest. She had nothing to protect her: security would never make it in time and she never came armed to work. There was nothing in her office that could fend off two attackers.

 

There was only one option for her. As discreetly as possible, Lena took her phone out and dialled Kara’s number. She held it to her ear, hyperaware that she was almost inside her office — a dead-end.

 

“Come on, come on,” Lena mumbled under her breath.

 

The moment she heard the footsteps speed up, Lena sprinted towards her office. She raced inside and locked the door behind her. Immediately, the attackers began pounding on the door.

 

Kara finally answered the call.

 

Quickly, Lena rushed out, “I’m at my office, please hurry.”

 

She hung up and pocketed her phone. In her panicked state, she found nothing in her office that could act as a weapon to defend herself — if she lived after this experience, she’d have to fix that oversight. So before they could break down the door, Lena rushed outside onto the balcony. She prayed that Kara made it in time.

 

It all happened so fast. One moment she was standing on the balcony as her attackers advanced. The next, there were hands on her body, grabbing and pulling. Finally, Lena felt herself fall back and then just keep falling. She looked up at the sky that was quickly becoming out of reach.

 

If this was Lena’s final moments, she refused to close her eyes.

 

But these were not her final moments. Just as quickly as she fell, something had caught Lena. Something warm and strong and oh so, familiar.

 

Lena saw the red cape and blue eyes before anything else. Supergirl had caught her and was still holding her in her arms, hovering above the concrete and asphalt below them. Supergirl had saved her.

 

Kara had saved her.

 

She flew them up and Lena took a moment to catch her breath. Brushes with death tended to throw you off, to say the least.

 

Kara hovered near the balcony and Lena smirked when her attackers stepped back in fear. Served them right. Kara set Lena down onto the balcony before turning around to face the men.

 

“Dropped something, boys?” Kara asked before knocking them unconscious.

 

Maybe it was just Lena but Kara seemed a bit excessive when she hit them. The grotesque crunching sound of Kara breaking their bones would probably stay with Lena for years after this moment.

 

“Let me deal with these two.” Kara said in a low voice. Lena shivered at the sound.

 

In one moment, Kara had flown off and had taken the two attackers with her. A beat later, Kara had returned empty-handed and began fussing over Lena. There were suddenly hands curled around Lena’s bicep and blue eyes scanning over her body for any injuries.

 

“Are you OK, Lena?” Kara asked, “Did they hurt you?”

 

Lena didn’t have time to process that she almost died just then. Into the little boxes this incident went, never to be unpacked or examined.

 

Shaking her head, Lena said, “No, no, I’m fine. You came just in time. Super-speed indeed.”

 

Her attempt at humour wasn’t received well: Kara didn’t even acknowledge it. Instead, Kara just pulled her into a hug and wrapped her cape around the two of them. It was almost as if Kara was using her cape to shield Lena from the world, if only for this moment in time. Lena found herself sinking into the embrace and wishing it never ended.

 

She wasn’t sure how long they stayed like that. All she knew was that when Kara pulled away and the cape fell from her shoulders, Lena felt impossibly cold.

 

“Were those Lex’s usual goons?” Kara asked, the rage in her voice barely contained.

 

Lena sighed, saying, “Most likely. He probably isn’t a fan of your new, shiny suit.”

 

She regretted the words the moment they left her mouth. Lena watched the way Kara’s rage was replaced with guilt and how her face just dropped. She wished she could take the words back but they just hung in the space between them, heavy and sickening.

 

“He…” Kara said, “he sent assassins after you because of what I said?”

 

Lena quickly said, “He would’ve found out eventually, even if you hadn’t said anything. I’m the only person smart enough and with the access to anti-Kryptonite technology to make the suit.”

 

Kara looked away and curled her hands into fists. Shaking, Kara breathed out, “I can’t seem to stop making life worse for people.”

 

Lena’s heart broke. She asked, “Woah, woah, where’s this coming from?”

 

She said nothing. Kara stared off into space and said nothing. Under the moonlight, Lena could see the unshed tears in Kara’s eyes.

 

Lena moved on instinct: she stepped forward and placed a hand on Kara’s cheek, forcing Kara to look at her. Kara wouldn’t meet her eyes, still.

 

“Darling, talk to me.” Lena pleaded.

 

Kara let out a shaky breath and seemed to be debating her next words. All Lena could do was wait with bated breath.

 

Finally, Kara said, “There’s a bunch of these aliens who escaped prison now flooding Earth. They’re the worst prisoners that Krypton could get their hands on and they’re all after me.”

 

“What? Why? You’ve done nothing wrong.” Lena said, feeling her breathing pick up.

 

Why would anyone want to harm Kara? Of all the vile, evil people in this universe, why hurt someone who only wanted to do good? Why hurt the best the universe had to offer? It made no sense to Lena.

 

“It’s because of my mother.” Kara said.

 

Lena couldn’t hear anything. Not the wind blowing against them, not the blood roaring in her ears, not her heart pounding in her chest. Only white noise that grew louder with every moment Kara didn’t speak.

 

Kara never talked about her family. It wasn’t about Kara being a private person or not trusting Lena; her family was tied directly to Kara’s trauma and could never be separated from it. Lena never probed Kara for information about them for good reason. Kara didn’t owe her memories to anyone.

 

So Kara bringing her up now was monumental to Lena.

 

“The prison is called Fort Rozz. It’s Krypton’s version of a maximum security prison. My mother put each and every one of those prisoners in there. She condemned them to the Phantom Zone and… and I have to wonder if it’s karmic retribution that her daughter ended up trapped there as well.” Kara said bitterly.

 

“Kara.” Lena breathed, trying to hold onto Kara with both hands.

 

Kara pulled away, careful not to hurt Lena even when she was so upset and unstable. The last time Kara had been this upset was directly after her fight with her family. Somehow, Lena knew that this wouldn’t end nearly as well for either of them

 

“You don’t understand.” Kara snarled, “There are fates worse than death. Those prisoners were in the Phantom Zone for years. Staring into pitch-black nothingness, unable to move or scream or cry. You don’t come back from something like that without something inside you dying, Lena.”

 

Lena didn’t know if Kara was still talking about those prisoners anymore. Kara had been in that pod for decades. How long did it take before the cryo-sleep took effect?
