A wicked grin grew on Kara’s face as she said, “I could hear your heartbeat across the city, Lena. It’s pounding.”

 

It was. It was threatening to leap out of Lena’s chest at this point. Be still, my beating heart .

 

Kara stood over her now. Lena hummed and placed her hands against Kara’s stomach, feeling the hard material under her. Kara cupped her face with both hands and murmured, “Do I make you nervous? Or is it excitement that’s getting your blood pumping right now?”

 

Fear. It was fear that consumed Lena now. Fear that Kara was going to break her beyond repair, or their friendship. She knew that if Kara had her way, she would consume Lena until she was satisfied — this wasn’t about Lena’s pleasure, really.

 

Instead of admitting this, Lena whispered, “Since when do you look for my heartbeat?”

 

Kara’s fingers were gentle as she caressed Lena’s face. If Lena wanted to, she could pretend like nothing was wrong and that she was simply taking a lover to bed. Kara’s hands moved from her face to her neck, her fingertips running along the ivory skin. The woman looked like she was admiring a priceless artwork and Lena wished it didn’t make her swoon like it did.

 

The spell was broken, however, when Kara said, “Since I stopped caring about hiding my powers.”

 

“Is that why you threw Cat Grant from a building?” Lena asked, testing the waters.

 

Kara let out a small laugh. Her hands moved back up until they entangled themselves in Lena’s hair.

 

“ That was to make a point.”

 

Lena remembered the dread that filled her body when the news had broken. Cat Grant thrown from a building by the hero that she claimed as hers — the irony wasn’t lost on Lena. And while Lena wasn’t fond of the woman, she was glad that Cat wasn’t dead. Kara would never be able to come back if she had killed someone. The mere thought of that made Lena feel sick.

 

“And what point is that, Kara?” Lena asked a little breathlessly.

 

The hands in Lena’s hair suddenly tightened and Lena’s head was pulled back. It was harsh and it hurt but Lena knew Kara could’ve used much more force. Small blessings, she supposed. Still, Lena hissed at the feeling and barely registered the voice in her ear.

 

In a voice as smooth as velvet, Kara said, “That there is one only power in this city. Me.”

 

And just like that, Kara let go and stepped back. Lena tried not to show how shaken up she was. She couldn’t fuck this up. Not when it was all going according to plan so far.

 

She noticed the way Kara’s eyes roamed over her body. Once, Lena would’ve given anything for Kara to look at her with such hunger and intensity. Now, Lena wished for the old Kara to come back. But she needed to play along until she could get her back.

 

“You do look powerful,” Lena smirked as she added, “in that suit I made for you.”

 

That flash of anger reappeared on Kara’s face. She snarled out, “I don’t need this suit, Lena.”

 

Too easy. Lena walked around her bed and draped herself across the sheets. Resting her head on her head, Lena hummed to herself and drawled, “Prove it.”

 

“You want me out of it that bad?” Kara asked, looking entirely too smug.

 

“I do.” Lena said with hooded eyes, “Come find out how badly.”

 

She turned her back to Kara. She could hear Kara murmur the deactivation phrase and the sound of the nanobots moving around. If she turned back now, she knew what she’d see. Lena slowly allowed her robe to slip down off her shoulders. She knew that her bare skin was on display and that Kara wouldn’t be able to resist touching the silk dress she wore.

 

True enough, she felt the bed dip behind her under Kara’s weight. It was now or never.

 

Kara’s hands were so large and heavy as they rested on Lena’s shoulders. Lena knew that she only had a small window of opportunity to act and it all hinged on her timing. Kara’s hands continued to dip lower down Lena’s back, feeling the dress and the flesh underneath it.

 

Time seemed to slow as Kara’s hands curled around Lena’s waist. As fast as Lena could, she grabbed the knife hidden in her robe and twisted her body around. Just before the knife could touch the skin of Kara’s chest, Lena’s wrist was grabbed and held in place.

 

Suddenly, Lena was looking up into the face of an angry alien who she just tried to stab. And not with just any knife. No, this knife emitted a soft, green glow that caused Kara to become enraged at the sight of it. Lena’s very own DEO-issued Kryptonite knife — the last thing Lena ever wanted to use against Kara.

 

“Kryptonite?” Kara snarled, “You were going to use kryptonite on me?”

 

Kara’s hand curled tighter around Lena’s wrist, hard enough to bruise now. Lena let out a cry of pain and struggled against her hold. If only she had been faster, she might’ve been able to at least nick Kara’s skin. But now she had failed and Kara looked murderous right now.

 

“Kara, please.” Lena pleaded, tears falling from her eyes.

 

Kara laughed mirthlessly at her, saying, “Did you think I wouldn’t notice you reaching for a knife? I’m much smarter than you, Luthor .”

 

Lena’s wrist felt like it was going to explode. No matter how hard she struggled, Kara wouldn’t move and she couldn’t slip out of her grasp. Kara had her exactly where she wanted her. Her life was in Kara’s hands now.

 

Thin red lines formed around Kara’s eyes like branches and there was a red glow to Kara’s eyes. In that moment, Lena was reminded of what Lex had said all those years ago: Devils don't come from hell beneath us. No, they come from the sky. There was no halo adorning Kara’s head tonight — there was only all-consuming rage and anguish inside Kara. Did Lucifer look like this as he fell from grace, as he lost everything that made him angelic?

 

Lena would never forget the hatred in Kara’s eyes, or how it was solely directed at Lena.

 

Kara yanked Lena forward by the wrist and said, “You humans are pathetic, little creatures and you Luthor’s are worse than scum!”

 

Lena just sobbed harder and struggled in vain because it was all she could do. Kara just sneered down at her and continued, “All those times I thought I saw potential in you. For once there was a Luthor that wasn’t completely out of their mind. But you turned out just like your brother, huh. Trying to kill a Super seems to run in the family.”

 

Her words cut deep and Lena tried to remember that Kara wasn’t herself right now. These weren’t really her words even if it came from her mouth. It didn’t make it hurt any less, though.

 

“You’re just a plaything to me.” Kara snarled, “You always have been. A pretty toy for me to lord around. Too bad I’ll have to kill you now.”

 

Lena shook her head and cried, “Kara, this isn’t you, you don’t kill!”

 

She struggled to see Kara through the tears blurring her vision and the pain that was making her dizzy. 

 

In a moment of deadly calm, Kara said, “I’ll make an exception for a Luthor.”

 

With that, Kara crushed Lena’s wrist until there was a sickening crunching sound. Lena screamed and nearly passed out from the sudden pain. Kara laughed and continued to squeeze, until Lena was sure she was going to faint. All she could focus on was Kara’s sadistic laughter echoing in her ears.

 

Then, there was a gunshot. Kara let out a roar and fell back against the bed, releasing Lena’s hand in the process. Lena scrambled away from the woman and cradled her wrist against her chest. She barely processed what was happening around her. She had seen Alex and a squad of agents enter the room, all armed with kryptonite-laced bullets. Had it been Alex who had shot Kara?

 

Lena didn’t remember too much after that. The last thing she had seen before she passed out was Kara donning the supersuit and flying off into the night.




later.




Red kryptonite. That’s what Alex called it, that… poison in Kara’s body. That poison was what corrupted everything good inside Kara and turned her into that monstrosity Lena saw today.

 

It was quite different from the green kryptonite considering this material was synthetic and took much longer to take effect on Kara’s body. It was likely a failed attempt at creating synthetic green kryptonite after it presented unpredictable results. How it managed to get anywhere near Kara at all was beyond Lena.

 

So much for the anti-kryptonite shields she had built into Kara’s suit. Clearly she had overestimated her abilities. Maybe if she hadn’t botched those shields, what happened to Kara might not have happened at all tonight.

 

What Lena did know was that the kryptonite alone was responsible for Kara’s behaviour, not Kara. She knew that the DEO had done their best to drag as much red kryptonite out of Kara and replace it with yellow solar radiation instead, similar to a blood-letting process. She also knew that her wrist ached, she was hungry and thirsty, her eyes hurt from crying and she was so damn tired.

 

But more than anything, Lena knew that she needed to see Kara.

 

Alex was staying with Kara until she regained consciousness. Lena respected their privacy and had been waiting outside the recovery room for the past few hours. Her phone was dying and she couldn’t remember the last time she ate anything, but she refused to leave: the thought of not being there as soon as Kara woke up was unbearable.

 

No, Lena would wait. She’d wait for the two Danvers sisters to have their moment alone. She’d wait the whole night if she had to.

 

As it turned out, she only needed to wait until midnight. Around half-past eleven, Lena heard the automatic doors to the recovery room hiss open. The doors parted to the side and out stepped Alex and Kara. Alex had her good arm wrapped around Kara’s shoulders as she led Kara outside. While there were no physical injuries, Kara looked like she had been through hell.

 

After tonight, it wouldn’t be wrong for her to feel like that.

 

Lena jumped to her feet and held in a wince when she jostled her wrist. The only thing on her mind was getting to Kara right now, pain be damned.

 

She took a few steps forward. Kara’s head snapped to the side and she seemed to freeze when she saw Lena. There was so much fear and pain in her eyes. It made Lena’s heart break just to look at. Thankfully, tears began to pool in her eyes so she didn’t have to look for long.

 

“Lena…” Kara murmured, the disbelief clear in her voice.

 

She slipped out of Alex’s grasp as she walked to Lena, her eyes never breaking contact as she did so. Lena blinked back tears as she held out her arms. At the sight of Lena’s outstretched arms, Kara’s face crumbled and she collapsed into Lena’s embrace. For a moment, Lena could pretend like the events of the day never happened. She could hug Kara and it was as if nothing had changed. Then Kara sobbed into Lena’s shoulder and she felt her heart break all over again.

 

“Shush, shush, I’m here,” Lena whispered as she rubbed Kara’s back with her good hand.

 

Lena could feel her wrist burning with pain from the sudden movement but she pushed through: Kara needed her right now.

 

From behind Kara, Alex was looking right at her. She caught Lena’s gaze for a moment. The two simply looked at each other for a while before Alex gave her a solemn nod. Before Alex could turn to leave, Lena mouthed “thank you” to her. She understood how hard it must’ve been for Alex to leave her little sister right now, especially in the company of a Luthor. It was progress, at least.

 

Kara pulled back and Lena couldn’t handle the pain any more as she cradled her wrist to her chest. She should’ve gone with the doctor when he offered her that pain relief. She had been too stubborn and refused to go anywhere, and she was paying for that stubbornness now.

 

“Your wrist…” Kara breathed out.

 

She looked horrified with herself. Lena could still remember the hatred she saw in Kara’s eyes as she squeezed Lena’s wrists, crushing the bone underneath. It was difficult to stop seeing that in Kara’s eyes, even now.

 

Lena grabbed onto Kara’s arm, saying, “The doctors already looked at it, I’ll be OK. It’s you I’m worried about.”

 

She pulled Kara towards the bench behind them and sat them down. The moment Kara sat down, she pulled away from Lena and buried her head in her hands. It was hard to believe that the woman who wanted to kill her tonight was the same woman in front of her, who looked so small and fragile.

 

Kara let out a sob, saying, “I hurt so many people.”

 

“You weren’t at fault,” Lena said gently, “it was the red kryptonite.”

 

Kryptonite that I should’ve protected you from , Lena thought bitterly.

 

“No, I should’ve been more careful. I threw Cat Grant off a building, I-I hurt you and Alex.” Kara said as her voice began to crack.

 

Lena placed her hand on Kara’s shoulder and she squeezed, earning a ragged breath from Kara. She asked, “Would you blame someone who had been involuntarily injected with heroin for their actions while drugged?”

 

Kara shook her head, saying, “It’s not the same.”

 

Lena smiled mirthlessly, saying, “You’re right. Red kryptonite is much worse than any drug could’ve been.”

 

Kara began to ramble as tears spilled down her cheeks, saying, “I’m so sorry, Lena. I didn’t mean any of it. The kryptonite made every bad thought I’ve ever had just come out. And I remember everything I did. Rao , the things I said, I never meant them, I swear—“

 

“I know, I know. It’s OK.” Lena cut her off as gently as she could.

 

She could imagine what it must feel like to go through that. Even for one day, Lena didn’t know what kind of damage she could do if she voiced every bad, intrusive thought she had. That didn’t mean that the things Kara said didn’t hurt. Lena would just have to add this entire day into one big box. Maybe the next time Lena had a bad day and drank herself blind, Kara’s voice would be added to the others in her head.

 

Kara looked at her with watery eyes, saying, “The kryptonite, it-it made me say things to hurt you. Only to hurt you. You’re not your brother, I—“

 

Lena’s hand moved before she could even think about it. She needed Kara to stop talking, to not say the one thing that Lena was trying to forget about the most. She suddenly had her uninjured hand cupping Kara’s cheek as she looked down at her.

 

“Kara,” she said firmly, “stop talking and look at me.”

 

The Kryptonian blinked away tears and sniffed but said nothing. A few hours ago, this face had been corrupted with red kryptonite and had been bearing down on Lena like a predator. For a moment, Lena remembered Lex and his stupid painting.

 

“Devils don't come from hell beneath us. No, they come from the sky.”

 

Kara had always been an angel in Lena’s eyes. The golden hair, the kind disposition, the way she inspired awe and wonder in the people around her — she was like a painting that had come to life, really. But tonight, Lena had seen another side of Kara. She saw a woman with crimson eyes and corruption running through her veins. She saw a woman who lorded her power over others and took pleasure in causing pain.

 

Lena supposed that even the devil himself had once been an angel. Maybe Lex had been wrong. Maybe there was an angel and devil inside everyone; an equal balance like all things in life.

 

What Lena did know for certain was that Kara Zor-El wasn’t in control tonight. The woman she saw tonight was simply a devil wearing Kara’s face, no more than that. The woman that Lena was looking at right now, the one who was hurting and who hated herself right now, was Kara.

 

Taking a deep breath, Lena said, “I love you.”

 

Kara’s face melted as she said, “I love you, too. So much.”

 

“Then take a deep breath for me.” Lena all-but ordered her.

 

Kara did as she was told. She took a deep, shuddering breath and pressed her face into Lena’s hand. Lena’s heart ached as she traced her thumb along Kara’s cheekbone.

 

Lena murmured, “Good.”

 

If their friendship was ever going to move on from this, Lena needed to be honest. The only thing she was dreading was the knowledge the truth would hurt Kara immeasurably.

 

“You scared me. You did more than scare me, actually.” Lena said, gesturing to her arm, “It’s hard to look at you and not see your face before you were about to… hurt me more.”

 

Kara couldn’t even look at her. She had her eyes screwed shut and Lena could feel her body shaking under her palm.

 

Lena continued, saying, “ But . We will work through this together. You weren’t in your right mind, how could I possibly hold this against you?”

 

“You should.” Kara forced out through clenched teeth.

 

Lena shook her head, saying, “That’s your guilt talking, darling.”

 

Kara opened her eyes. They always had been Lena’s weakness, especially when they looked at Lena with so much agony.

 

“We won’t be the same… not after tonight.” Lena murmured with a bitter taste in her mouth.

 

Kara looked like she was about to cry again before Lena continued, saying, “But we’ll get past this. You’re not getting rid of me that easy, Kara Zor-El.”

 

Lena’s sad smile wasn’t returned.

 

Instead, Kara rasped out, “I can’t lose you.”

 

Her voice was so small, so broken. Lena was glad that the feeling was mutual, at least.

 

She pressed a kiss to Kara’s forehead that lingered for a little too long. She knew that Kara didn’t share her feelings: the kryptonite made Kara want to toy with Lena’s emotions. Kara said so herself that she meant nothing that she had done tonight. That was the sad part, wasn’t it? That Lena was grateful there was no truth to Kara likening her to her brother, but that meant that there was no truth behind her flirting and advances.

 

Kara would never feel the same. Tonight was just the final nail in the coffin for Lena’s heart. Shovuh , never zhao . A Luthor and a Super were never meant to be. No matter how much Lena’s heart yearned for it.

 

Leaning away, Lena said, “You never will. Not over this.”

 

Any kind of love was better than no love at all.

 

The two of them sat on that bench wrapped up in each other for a long time. Lena had forgotten about her hunger and pain as she sat with Kara, finally feeling at ease. The broken wrist sitting in Lena’s lap that throbbed with pain could wait.

 

Kara seemed entirely interested in Lena’s wrist, however. She kept poking and fiddling with the splint, occasionally playing with Lena’s fingers as well. Lena just let it happen: it was times like this when she remembered just how much younger Kara was.

 

Quietly, Kara asked, “Are you getting a cast for this?”

 

Lena didn’t need to have a genius level intellect to hear the guilt in her voice. Well-deserved guilt, yes, but it still made Lena’s heart clench.

 

“One of the doctors here said they’ll do it.” Lena hummed and asked, “Have any colours in mind?”

 

Without missing a beat, Kara looked up at Lena and said, “Green. It’s always been your best colour.”

 

Lena couldn’t stop the smile that grew on her face if she wanted to.

 

“Green it is.”

 

They were going to be just fine.

i hope i hurt someone's feelings with this one.

8. viii.

“Miss Luthor, do you have any comment on Supergirl’s frenzy last week?”

 

“Can Supergirl be trusted? Did she do that to your arm?”

 

“Are the rumours true, Lena? Are you and Supergirl dating?”

 

“The public has a right to know! What happened to Supergirl?”

 

Journalists had been clamouring over themselves outside of L-Corp since last week. The security guards had done their best to fend off these ravenous beasts but there was little they could do to stop their voices.

 

The only answer they received was a loud sigh from the CEO of L-Corp. Lena knew better than to give these people the time of day: the truth never really mattered to them, only what they could use for their own agendas. And as far as she was concerned, they didn’t deserve the truth either.

 

Security escorted Lena through the crowd and to the car parked in front of the building. It was only until the car door shut beside her and the car began to move, that Lena was able to breathe again.

 

Her driver, Santiago, heard the sigh and said, “Are you alright, Miss Luthor?”

 

“Bloody vultures,” Lena grumbled, “the lot of them.”

 

He seemed to understand Lena’s poor mood and graciously didn’t initiate a conversation. Lena was allowed to watch the city pass by through the window, feeling the stress of the day begin to leave her.

 

It was supposed to be an early night for Lena. She had a meeting the next morning that she absolutely couldn’t miss and then plenty of tasks to keep her busy (and frustrated) for the rest of the day. All Lena wanted was to curl into bed and sleep for a week straight.

 

Instead, she’d likely go home, drink some tea and sleep for a few hours at most. She just couldn’t seem to get more than a few hours of sleep these days.

 

So that’s why Lena was now standing in her kitchen, dressed in her pyjamas and dipping the tea bags into her cup. She could feel the weariness in her bones and the heaviness in her eyelids. Soon, she could slip into bed. If only she—

 

Knock, knock.

 

Lena’s head whipped around so she could look at the door. Who could possibly be knocking at her door at this time of the night? Surely it couldn’t be Kara: she would’ve knocked on the balcony window.

 

With the worst possible scenarios playing out in her mind, Lena made her way to the door and activated the tablet perched on the wall beside it. The screen showed exactly who was standing outside her door and, to her surprise, it was Kara. Lena didn’t hesitate to open her door to her.

 

Kara was dressed in a hoodie, slacks and boots. Her hair was tied up in a bun and she was wearing those dorky glasses that Lena was so fond of.

 

With a smile and a confused expression, Lena said, “Kara?”

 

For a moment, Lena wondered if she had forgotten that she invited Kara over. When she realised that she did no such thing she wondered why Kara was here exactly. Lena was glad to see her, of course, but she was still confused.

 

There was still that tinge of guilt that Lena felt whenever she thought of Kara, now. She still looked at Kara and saw the woman who nearly killed her, yes, but she also believed that that whole incident was her fault. If her anti-kryptonite shields hadn’t failed, Kara wouldn’t have had to go through all of that.

 

She would never mention this guilt to Kara, however. She could practically hear the speech about Lena being good and undeserving of criticism, especially from herself. No, she would keep this to herself and quietly make plans for a supersuit overhaul in future.

 

In the present, Kara was looking at Lena like a deer in headlights. Her mouth was hanging open and she seemed rooted where she stood. Actually, Lena noticed that Kara’s eyes were drifting between her face and her chest. Lena was wearing a shirt so there was no chance she was showing any cleavage. So what was the issue?

 

“You, uh,” Kara trailed off, a red tint forming on her cheeks.

 

The two of them were standing awkwardly in the doorway now. Kara was opening and closing her mouth like a fish, and Lena wasn’t sure what to say.

 

Eventually, Kara shook her head and said, “I just wanted to see you.”

 

Lena’s heart swelled at these words. She stepped to one side and gestured for Kara to come inside. Kara slipped past her, headed straight for the living room couch.

 

“You OK?” Lena asked, shutting the door behind her.

 

Kara nearly jumped out of her skin and said, “Yes! Yes, I’m fine.”

 

“Right,” Lena said with a gentle smile, “that totally wasn’t a lie.”

 

The Kryptonian let out a small laugh and looked down at the ground, avoiding her eyes. Lena sighed and walked towards her, slow in her gait. Noticing her advance, Kara glanced up at Lena before looking away. Lena watched her fidget with her glasses and that was all she needed to know, really.

 

“Kara.” Lena said, a hint of sterness in her voice.

 

She sighed and murmured, “You’re going to think it’s dumb.”

 

Lena gave a small smile, saying, “Tell me anyways.”

 

Kara leaned back against the couch, running her palm back and forth along its surface. Lena was so curious as to what was going on with Kara but she kept quiet as she waited.

 

“You’re wearing my shirt. The crest…” Kara trailed off, glancing at Lena’s shirt.

 

Her shirt? Lena looked down and everything clicked into place. She was wearing a super shirt. The famous ‘S’ was printed in white onto Lena’s otherwise black shirt. She had bought the shirt on a whim when she saw it on sale online. Beyond her own personal reasons for wearing the symbol, Lena just wanted a new shirt to wear and it had been available.

 

Still, she hadn’t prepared for this exact scenario in which Kara would catch her wearing it. Lena began to feel embarrassed despite having done nothing wrong.

 

Clearing her throat, Lena asked, “Is that a problem? I thought you’d appreciate it.”

 

Kara was quick to say, “I do! I do, it’s just…”

 

There was that awkwardness again. Lena felt like a fish out of water now. Was it OK to wear this shirt or not?

 

“You... wearing that shirt,” Kara said and adjusted her glasses, “It’s… Wearing another family’s crest is a sign of marriage on Krypton.”

 

“Oh.”

 

Lena had been floored. Of all possible answers, that was the last one she had anticipated. Despite herself, Lena could feel her cheeks heating up at a rapid pace.

 

Kara’s face was bright red to match now, saying, “Yeah.”

 

Marriage. Oh God, this was a nightmare. Surely this wasn’t a binding thing, right? Lena didn’t wear the shirt for that purpose so maybe it didn’t count. Still, Lena’s heart rate began to pick up at the thought of being married to Kara.

 

“I mean, it’s not so bad. Lots of people wear my family’s crest and I’m not married to them, right?” Kara said, a toothy grin on her face.

 

That was true. It was common to see people wearing the famous symbol on their clothing, considering how famous the heroes who they represented were.

 

Lena licked at her lips and asked, “Does it make you uncomfortable?”

 

“You wearing the crest?” Kara asked, glancing between Lena and the shirt.

 

Lena nodded, saying, “And the rest of the world.”

 

Kara ran her hand through her hair and sighed. Lena took a moment to look at Kara and wondered if she had always been so thin. Was she eating properly? Was she overworking herself as Supergirl? When was the last time Kara had a day off between university and her hero duties? Lena felt a rush of concern flood through her body.

 

“I was angry the first time I saw someone wearing that shirt. It’s a crime to wear another House’s crest as your own on Krypton. It’s one of the most disrespectful things you could do, really. Kind of like stolen valour.” Kara said, a dark look in her eyes as she spoke.

 

Lena swallowed thickly. The last thing she had wanted to do was hurt Kara.

 

“I’m sorry, Kara,” she said softly.

 

Kara smiled and wrapped her hands around Lena’s arms, rubbing them in an attempt to comfort Lena.

 

“No, no, it’s OK.” Kara said, “Eventually, I decided I was fine with it. Yes, it was weird to see strangers wearing that shirt but they weren’t wearing it to disrespect my family. They wore it because they believed in Superman and they’re proud to wear his symbol.”

 

Not everyone did it for Superman. Lena certainly didn’t.

 

With a far away look on her face, Kara said, “In a weird way, having people wear the crest feels like a part of Krypton lives on.”

 

What could Lena possibly say to that? Nothing of substance, she decided. There weren’t enough words in the world to soothe the sorrow that that statement held.

 

Instead, Lena frowned and asked, “You sure you’re OK with me wearing it?”

 

Kara’s smile was impossibly bright as she said, “One hundred percent.”

 

To say Lena was relieved was an understatement. Kara let go of Lena’s arms and settled herself on the couch as she had done countless times before. When had Lena gotten so used to the sight of Kara in her home? Had it really been almost a year since they met? That night in the rain seemed like it had happened a lifetime ago for Lena.

 

God, Lena was getting old and sentimental.

 

“And just to set the record straight, Kara,” Lena said as she rounded the couch.

 

Kara looked up from where she sat on the couch with those big, blue eyes that never failed to draw her in. Lena’s heart clenched at the sight of them.

 

She stood in front of Kara and reached down to tilt her chin up. Now, the two of them were locked in each other’s gaze. Lena smiled and said, “Superman isn’t the one whose symbol I’m proud to wear. It’s yours.”

 

Kara’s lips parted and her eyes grew wide. Lena swore she could see them twinkle under the light. After a moment, Kara wore a bashful smile and murmured, “Thank you.”

 

When Lena was sure that her heart was going to burst, she took her hand away and sat beside Kara on the couch. The scent of laundry detergent clung to Kara and it was somehow calming for Lena.

 

In an attempt to remove the awkwardness, Lena said, “If it helps, I can get you an L-Corp shirt to wear. Might even the playing field.”

 

Kara looked startled and said, “Then we’d definitely be married. By Kryptonian standards anyway.”

 

Good job, Lena. Now you’ve made things even more awkward and you’re thinking about marrying Kara. Again .

 

“How’s L-Corp doing?” Kara asked, breaking the silence between them.

 

“Busy as always. Sometimes I think I should just permanently move into my office. Might get more sleep if I do,” Lena murmured before draping the throw blanket across both of their laps.

 

“Maybe if you just went home earlier like I ask you to…” Kara said, flashing her a look of reprimand.

 

Lena rolled her eyes, saying, “If I leave early, there will still be work to be done when I come back the next day. And I’d rather not bring my work back home with me.”

 

Kara frowned and jutted out her bottom lip, looking dangerously cute. The urge to kiss away the pout was almost overwhelming and Lena had to look away before it took over.

 

“Don’t pout at me.” Lena said, pushing her by the shoulder. She received a grin after this.

 

The two of them settled into quiet conversation. The awkwardness from earlier had mostly disappeared and while Lena was still tired, she enjoyed Kara’s company too much to leave. Kara was worth losing a few hours of sleep for, anyway.

 

Eventually, Kara said, “Sometimes I wish you didn’t change the company name.”

 

Lena raised an eyebrow and said, “Oh, yeah?”

 

Kara pulled her glasses off and Lena could feel that flutter in her chest as she drank in the sight. God, her bare face was gorgeous. Not even the greatest of Michaengelo’s sculptures could captivate Lena’s as strongly as Kara’s face could. Rao took his time when he created her it seemed.

 

“Yeah. Luthor Corp had a nice ring to it.” Kara said as she set the glasses on the coffee table.

 

Lena scoffed, saying, “The Luthor name doesn’t deserve the recognition. Not after everything my family has done.”

 

Kara shot her a sad look which Lena just ignored. She hadn’t said anything wrong, so that frown wasn’t necessary. After everything the Luthor family had done, Lena’s decision had been the only acceptable choice. Hers was a cursed name and it would never taint the work she did. She promised herself that a long time ago.

 

“Well, it can’t be that bad.” Kara said, “Not if it has you.”

 

Lena looked at her sharply. Kara held her gaze, unwavering and full of conviction.

 

Sighing, Lena said, “You think too highly of me.”

 

Kara shook her head and argued, “Not highly enough.”

 

There was no arguing with Kara when it came to this topic. For whatever reason, Kara had appointed herself as the fiercest of Lena’s supporters and refused to tolerate any slight against her name, even if it came from Lena herself. What Lena did to deserve such devotion, she didn’t know.

 

And whether Kara’s behaviour made her heart swell was irrelevant.

 

There was a lull in conversation. Lena could feel her eyes fluttering shut and decided it would be alright to close them, just for a little bit. Kara wouldn’t mind. She never did. Sweet, gentle Kara who held oceans in her eyes.

 

Lena felt warm fingers running along her own fingers. They were gentle as they moved and Lena knew what Kara was going to say next.

 

“How’s the cast?” Kara asked, the tension in her voice clear.

 

Lena kept her eyes closed as she hummed, saying, “It’ll heal. That’s all the matters.”

 

The fingers moved lower. Now they were tracing along Lena’s palm, mapping out the cold skin there with care. Quietly, Kara asked, “Does it hurt?”

 

Sighing, Lena opened her eyes and caught Kara’s gaze.

 

“Kara, I’m fine.” Lena said firmly, “Stop beating yourself up.”

 

She received no response. The fingers continued to trace up and down her hand, keeping the same slow pace. Kara was avoiding her eyes once more. Lena wished she could read Kara’s mind and figure out what was eating away at her. Was the red kryptonite incident still fresh in her mind? If it hadn’t left Lena’s thoughts, surely it hadn’t left Kara’s.

 

With her uninjured hand, Lena reached out and held Kara’s hand. No more tracing.

 

Lena asked, “You sure you’re alright, Kara?”

 

No answer. Kara looked away and as she turned, a stray lock of blonde hair fell into her face. Lena’s breath hitched in her chest. She reached up and tucked the hair behind Kara’s ear before she could stop herself.

 

Kara’s gaze turned to look at Lena in surprise. Lena was surprised at herself, honestly. She wasn’t sure what possessed her to do that. Still, the look in Kara’s eyes told her the gesture wasn’t unwelcome.

 

“Can I ask you for a favour?” Kara asked, her voice quiet even at this distance.

 

Lena didn’t hesitate to say, “Just name it.”

 

Whatever Kara wanted, whether it was money, attention, comfort, a shoulder to lean on — if Lena could give it to Kara, she would in a heartbeat. 

 

With a hint of bashfulness, Kara asked, “Do you mind if I sleep here tonight?”

 

Lena blinked. Was that all? Surely there was more to this favour. It wasn’t like Kara hadn’t slept over before; the guest bedroom was Kara’s unofficial room here. She didn’t need to ask to sleep over.

 

“Of course.” Lena said, adding, “Can I ask why?”

 

“I just…” Kara trailed off and chewed on her bottom lip.

 

Lena reached over and squeezed Kara’s hand, hoping to provide some comfort through her touch.

 

“After the whole red kryptonite thing, I…” Kara murmured, “I’ve been getting nightmares. They keep me up at night. I can’t exactly just pop a sleeping pill. I just… I just wanna stop feeling like this.”

 

Lena’s heart clenched painfully at these words. Kara looked so small as she spoke and part of Lena wanted to pull the woman into her arms, if only to shield her from her nightmares.

 

Instead, Lena said, “You know you can talk to me.”

 

Kara gave her a soft smile and murmured, “I know.”

 

No , Lena thought, you don’t know the half of it, my dear .

 

“When I was younger, I used to get nightmares a lot. This was after I landed here. I wasn’t used to sleeping and—“ Kara was cut off.

 

“Wait. What do you mean you weren’t used to sleeping?” Lena asked, a frown on her face.

 

The rosy blush returned to Kara’s face as she said, “Kryptonians don’t actually need to sleep. We don’t have a circadian rhythm like you humans do. We’re designed to survive on sunlight, so food and sleep weren’t exactly needed to function. All we needed was Rao , our sun. So when the winter months came and we didn’t get as much sunlight anymore, we could choose to nest for a week to conserve some energy. That is, if we didn’t want to use solar stores which were considered precious resources.”

 

That was a lot of information to process for Lena. Still, this was new information about Krypton and Lena wanted to learn everything she could about this planet and its people.

 

“Nest?” Lena asked.

 

Kara whined and buried her head into her hands. Her voice was muffled as she said, “Ah, this is kind of embarrassing.”

 

Lena couldn’t help the small laugh that came out of her. It wasn’t her fault that Kara was so cute when she was flustered.

 

“You know how bears hibernate during the winter? Well, Kryptonians go into a state of torpor. It’s not as extreme or long as hibernating, and we can still be woken up from it. Just think of it like an extended nap.” Kara said, not able to hold Lena’s gaze for long.

 

Lena asked, “And this is called nesting?”

 

Kara said, “Yeah. Because we would actually build a nest. Like a giant pillow fort in our bedrooms.”

 

Lena gave her a stern look, saying, “You’re just messing with me now.”

 

“I’m serious.” Kara replied, without a hint of humour.

 

Lena wasn’t even sure how to process this. She said, “Wait, so if you don’t need to sleep, then why…”

 

Kara said, “It’s just a habit at this point. I really don’t need to sleep thanks to the yellow sun but sleeping is nice. It’s a good way to let my body rest after a long day. And it technically saves my energy, so, yeah.”

 

Thoughts of Kara building a giant nest and being tucked away in there for days was… a little adorable, if Lena was being honest. It was such a shame that Earth’s yellow sun provided Kara with more than enough energy to no longer need to nest.

 

“You were telling me about when you were younger,” Lena said, “before I interrupted you.”

 

“Right. Well, when I first landed here, I had to learn how to sleep like humans did. Alex used to sleep in the same room as me and she used to get so mad if I tried staying up. I never told her that I tried staying up to distract myself from…” Kara began to trail off, that far-away look returning to her face.

 

From what, Lena wondered. From her time in the Phantom Zone? From having to watch her planet explode and lose her people in one moment? From remembering her family on Krypton and feeling the pain of losing them all over again? There was more than enough trauma for Kara to take her pick, really.

 

Kara shook her head and said, “Anyway, I got used to sleeping near someone else. Hearing Alex’s heart beating used to make it easy to fall asleep.”

 

“So why not sleep with Alex?” Lena asked.

 

Don’t get her wrong: Lena was more than happy to have Kara sleep over again. But she still had to ask, considering that Alex was probably Kara’s favourite person on Earth. Why Lena?

 

“I don’t want her to worry because my nightmares have come back. She’s probably with that detective anyway. And besides, your heart beat is familiar. I’ll sleep just as easy with you nearby.” Kara admitted, giving Lena a small smile.

 

Lena was definitely going to faint if she didn’t remove herself from this conversation. She hoped she didn’t look as flustered as she felt.

 

“You can stay for as long as you want, Kara,” Lena murmured, avoiding eye contact.

 

She heard Kara let out a small laugh before saying: “Thanks, Lena.”

 

It was late. Lena had a long day at work tomorrow and she was exhausted. Honestly, she should’ve been asleep by now but she could never turn down Kara. Not that she even wanted to, anyway.

 

She should just throw the blanket off of her lap, say goodnight and head to bed. She should do that. But there was one burning question on her mind and she couldn’t ignore it. 

 

Lena gave her a coy smile and asked, “Familiar, huh?”

 

There was no jest in Kara’s voice when she spoke. Instead, Kara’s voice was tender as she said, “Comforting might be a better word.”

 

Lena knew that she was red in the face now. Gosh, did she have to say these kinds of things? Didn’t she know that Lena’s heart was frail and prone to stuttering around a certain honey-tongued Kryptonian?

 

But if Lena was being honest with herself, she would admit that the sentiment was shared: she would sleep easier at night if she knew that Kara slept under the same roof, safe and sound.




later.



Never in Lena’s wildest dreams had she imagined being blindfolded by Kara. Yet here she was, sitting in Kara’s apartment with a blindfold wrapped around her eyes. Well, it wasn’t exactly a blindfold considering it was just one of Kara’s ties. Still, it served its function because Lena couldn’t see a damned thing.

 

Lena huffed and asked, “Will you tell me why I’m being blindfolded now?”

 

Kara’s voice came from a distance, saying, “I’m almost done!”

 

Done with what exactly? The only thing Lena could think of was that Kara had picked up some stray animal from the street, which would be a nightmare for Lena. Mostly because the filthy creature would take Kara’s attention away from Lena herself.

 

Instead of voicing this, Lena whined, “I can’t believe you flew me across the city and you won’t even give me a hint as to what I’m doing here.”

 

“I told you it was a surprise!” Kara yelled back, sounding giddy.

 

Lena sighed in defeat. She supposed she had no choice but to wait. She had no doubt that whatever Kara was going to show her was going to be worth the wait. God, please don’t be a pet.

 

Suddenly, Lena felt hands drop onto her shoulders. She had to refrain from yelping out of fright and only managed by a slight margin.

 

“Ready?” Kara asked, squeezing Lena’s shoulders.

 

Lena nodded. She felt Kara’s hands leave her shoulders to begin untying the blindfold. In a few moments, Lena could see again and she gasped at the sights laid out in front of her.

 

There were so many paintings on display. None of them were of the same thing and there were countless colours used. Exquisite detail put into these paintings, each unique and captivating. Clearly expert hands had created these artworks. Lena had been to plenty of art exhibitions and museums in her life, but these paintings were something entirely different.

 

Kara spoke up from behind her, saying, “I finished a few paintings. These are the ones I’m happy with, anyway.”

 

“Kara…” Lena was at a loss for words.

 

She stood up and found herself moving on autopilot. Her eyes scanned each of the paintings, drinking them in one by one. After a moment, Lena’s eyes settled on one that depicted an orb.

 

No. Not an orb, Lena realised.

 

Quietly, Lena asked, “Is this Krypton?”

 

She didn’t have to look at Kara to know that she was smiling when she said: “It is.”

 

Lena’s heart clenched. The painting showed Krypton as it would be viewed from space. It had a similar structure to Earth with oceans separating the lands and connected continents. There was a careful use of colour from the blues of the ocean to the reds and greens of the earth. She could feel how ancient this planet was just by looking at it.

 

She felt Kara brush up against her. Lena didn’t take her eyes off of the art. She reached out and touched the part of the painting that looked like a mistake — it should’ve been an ocean but it looked wrong, somehow.

 

Kara murmured, “That’s the boiling sea. I’ve only ever seen it from a distance. Legend says that the sea was created when Rao gazed upon his people during a civil war and he burned so hot from rage that the sea would boil forevermore as a reminder: Rao would never want his people’s blood spilt.”

 

Lena’s heart was singing in her chest. This was Krypton , Kara’s home. It’s where her people had lived and where Kara had grown up. This was an entire civilisation immortalised on this canvas.

 

Her finger drifted across the paint until it landed on a red patch, standing in contrast to the green land surrounding it.

 

“That’s the fire falls. A geological marvel. All those magma streams just… perfectly symmetrical with each other,” Kara said.

 

Lena wanted to linger on this painting a little more. She wanted to explore every inch, every colour, and listen to every story that Kara told her. However, another painting caught her attention and she drifted towards it.

 

The colours were mostly blues and whites, everything in cool tones. At first, Lena wasn’t sure what she was looking at. It looked like a forest but no foliage Lena had ever seen came in that colour. There was no green which meant no chlorophyll so how could it be a forest? The longer Lena looked, the more she realised what the painting depicted. 

 

“Is that glass?” Lena asked, her eyes widening.

 

“The forest of glass. It had been an accident caused by the Science Guild — an oversight during an experiment. One thing led to another and an explosion caused… that. It looks like a forest of glass. It was a hundred years old, if I remember it right.” Kara said, her voice thick with emotion.

 

Lena turned to look at her. Sometimes Lena forgot that Kara was an alien. She forgot that Kara belonged to a completely different world and had lived a life foreign to anything Lena could understand. And the only memories of this forgotten world lived on in Kara.

 

And now they were immortalised in these paintings.

 

There was so much to look at. Kara mostly painted landscapes and cities; it was so interesting to see the city of Kandor and all its’ citizens. There were people wearing robes woven from materials Lena couldn’t even imagine, and structures that didn’t look structurally possible. Yet these had all been real — they had been Kara’s home.

 

Lena wandered around the room. Her eyes landed on a thick, leather-bound journal that sat on Kara’s desk. She could see pages peeking out and the colours that stained them.

 

She placed her hand on the cover and felt the worn pages under her touch. She looked back at Kara and asked, “Can I look at this?”

 

“Go ahead. It’s mostly sketches and water colours,” Kara said, licking at her lips.

 

Lena felt like a child on Christmas morning as she opened up the journal. She began to flick through the pages, taking in all the sketches that were there. Kara truly had a gift for the fine arts: the animals she drew seemed so life-like and the water colours that Kara used made them feel even more real.

 

There were mostly animals that she drew. Lions, tigers and bears, and much more had graced these pages. Lena felt herself smiling as she flicked through the pages.

 

“These are wonderful,” Lena murmured.

 

She turned the page and paused. No matter how hard Lena tried, she couldn’t seem to figure out what exactly she was looking at.

 

She pointed at the image and said, “I don’t recognise this animal.”

 

In an instant, Kara’s chest was pressed up against Lena’s back. Lena felt her head hovering over Lena’s shoulder as the Kryptonian examined the sketch she was pointing at. Lena felt light-headed at the sudden proximity.

 

Kara laughed softly and said, “That’s because it’s not from this planet.”

 

Lena’s eyes grew wide. She turned her head slightly to look at Kara, saying, “I thought there were no animals on Krypton.”

 

“There weren’t. I told you I’ve visited other planets,” Kara said, a twinkle of mischief in her eyes.

 

As if being pressed against Kara’s chest wasn’t bad enough, Kara’s arm came around and she began pointing out certain animals. Lena tried not to let herself be distracted by the sight of Kara’s hands.

 

“That’s a marine animal from the planet Naktos. Basically a tortoise but with a more angular shell and sharp teeth.” Kara said as her finger slid down the page, “And this is a Thoron domesticated pet. It’s like a mix between a coyote and hyena but they’re super friendly.”

 

“So it’s like a domesticated dog,” Lena deadpanned.

 

Kara said, “Yup. It’s so cool how you guys domesticated wolves into dogs.”

 

Absentmindedly, Lena said, “We’re doing it with foxes now, too.”

 

“What?” Kara had all-but yelped out.

 

Lena ignored her in favour of leafing through the pages. Her head was swimming with all this new information. The scientist inside her was screaming. She wondered what kind of conditions caused these animals to evolve into what they were. She wondered what ecosystem they lived in and how they contributed to them. She wondered about their diets, their behaviours, everything about them.

 

There were whole worlds out there that Lena knew nothing about. But Kara did and she was willing to share them with her. This was the greatest gift she could’ve received.

 

Curious, Lena asked, “What kind of pet would you want, Kara?”

 

There was a moment of silence before Kara said, “I’ve always wanted a dog. I mean, I had Streaky and he was the best cat ever. But I’ve never had a dog before.”

 

Lena smiled. She just knew that Kara probably wanted the biggest, fluffiest dog available. She would’ve gotten one for Kara if it weren’t for the fact that she was in no position to care for a pet right now. Between her Supergirl duties and being a university student, Kara just wouldn’t have the time.

 

One day, though.

 

Kara asked, “What about you?”

 

Lena turned her head to the side. So did Kara, and the two were suddenly face to face. Lena could feel Kara’s breath on her lips and it sent an electric shock down her body. Instinctively, Lena moved back to get some distance between them. Kara coughed and moved back as well, a red tint to her cheeks.

 

Trying to talk herself out of the awkwardness, Lena said, “I don’t want a pet. Too high maintenance and I’m not interested in picking up after them.”

 

“But they’re so cute.” Kara said, a slight whine in her voice.

 

Lena rolled her eyes and said, “From a distance, yes.”

 

Silence settled between them once more. Lena set the journal down and turned her attention to the rest of the room. How long had it taken Kara to create all of this? Months? Years? Part of Lena wanted these artworks to be displayed at a museum or an exhibition for the world to see and appreciate — a larger part of Lena knew that they were sacred to Kara, and only people she trusted should have the privilege to view them.

 

Her heart fluttered when she realised that, yes, she was one of the people that Kara trusted to see these.

 

Turning around to look at her, Lena said, “Kara, this is all so amazing.”

 

Kara walked across the room as she spoke, saying, “There’s something else I wanted to show you.”

 

Lena watched with bated breath. Was this one of those ‘leave the best of last’ times? What was so special that Kara wanted to show it Lena personally? Her thoughts were going a thousand miles an hour, second only to her heart beating in her chest.

 

Kara picked up a canvas that had been resting away from the rest of the artworks. It had been facing the wall so Lena didn’t know what was on it. As Kara walked towards the empty easel, Lena tried to catch a glimpse but still couldn’t decipher it.

 

It was only until Kara placed it on the easel that Lena realised what it was. Her heart dropped into her stomach.

 

“This,” Kara said, her eyes never leaving the painting, “is the House of El.”

 

It was a family portrait. Kara’s family. To the left was a tall, bearded man who had an impossibly warm smile. He wore a black robe with a red cape draped over one shoulder. The crest on his house was carved into the spaulder, impossible to miss. Beside him stood his wife. She wore a white robe with a high neck, with her brown hair tied up. Her crest was displayed as an armband of gold that curled around her bicep. The sides of both parents heads’ were grey from age and it looked like it had been maintained as a fashion statement.

 

And in between them, wearing a familiar smile, was Kara. She was so young in this image. Her hair was shorter and her cheeks were rounder. Kara had found the perfect shade to fill in those blue eyes. She wore a robe similar to her father’s and a thin diadem that rested on her head. When Lena drew closer, she realised that the centrepiece of the diadem had the crest woven into it.

 

“Kara…” Lena found herself lost for words.

 

Running her hand down the side of the canvas, Kara said, “The man with the thick beard was my father, Zor-El. He was a doctor. Then there’s my mother, Alura Zor-El. She was a judge and a part of the Religious Guild. It was said that only those who were blessed by Rao and who walked in his light could judge his people. It was quite the controversy when she married my father, who was from a lower caste.”

 

“The Science Guild,” Lena said, recalling their previous conversations.

 

Kara looked elated that Lena remembered. She nodded, saying, “Mhm. But since the difference in caste positions wasn’t too big, they were allowed to choose each other.”

 

Lena tore her gaze away from the painting to ask, “Choose each other to marry?”

 

“Technically, to reproduce.” Kara clarified, adding, “Kryptonians don’t marry for love, remember? When they are created by the Growth Codex, they already have a list of possible candidates that they are compatible with. When a Kryptonian becomes of age, they are allowed to choose a candidate to court or outright marry.”

 

Lena frowned at this. Before a person was even born or had the chance to develop their own values and personality, their life is decided for them. It’s no way to live your life. God, she couldn’t imagine Kara being forced to marry rather than doing so of her own volition.

 

“What if you loved someone who wasn’t one of the candidates?” Lena asked.

 

Kara shrugged and said, “Then you could take them as a paramour. They were quite common since most marriages were ones of necessity, rather than love. But you can only marry one of those candidates.”

 

Grimacing, Lena said, “I wouldn’t want to live like that.”

 

Kara’s hand curled around Lena’s shoulder and squeezed gently. Kara said, “You never will. You can find someone, fall in love and give them your last name. No all-knowing codex to decide for you.”

 

There was no malice or condescension in her voice. If anything, Kara sounded yearning . Lena wanted to ask her so much. Who would Kara have married? Would she marry someone for love, or take a paramour? Had Kara already known some of the candidates? There was so much she wanted to ask but knew better than to actually act upon this desire.

 

Instead, Lena scoffed and said, “Believe me, no one would ever take my surname.”

 

The hand on Lena’s shoulder loosened. There was a pause. Then, Kara asked, “Why wouldn’t they?”

 

The way she asked that question made Lena want to cry. Why couldn’t Kara just understand ? Everyone else in the world would understand why, except Kara.

 

“I’m a Luthor, Kara. And I know that you believe that everything is good and kind, and that... that is one of the things I love about you. But the world isn’t kind. No one wants a cursed name.” Lena said, unable to keep the venom out of her voice.

 

The hand fell from her shoulder. Lena didn’t want to turn around. She didn’t want to look back and see Kara’s sad eyes. She didn’t want to look at someone who believed in her more than she believed in herself. If she did, she knew that she would cry.

 

Still, Lena felt Kara move behind her. Kara stood shoulder to shoulder with Lena and looked up at her parents with a soft smile.

 

And then she began to speak.

 

“When my mother chose my father, everyone warned her not to take my father’s name. In-Ze was a good name, a blessed name. It was because of superstition that said taking the surname of a lower caste parent would cause the child to have bad luck. Rao , status was everything to my people,” Kara said with a shake of her head.

 

Lena turned to look at Kara, while Kara remained focused on the painting.

 

She continued, saying, “So when I first learned about all this, I went home and I asked them why. Why did my mother choose to take my father’s name? Why did I have my father’s name and not hers?”

 

Not even Lena knew the answer to this. It would’ve made more sense for Kara to become an In-Ze if it was a better name. So why?

 

Kara finally looked at her. She smiled and said, “Because she loved him. She saw all the good he did for his people and how he would work himself to death if it meant one more of his patients rested easy that night. She said that she was proud to wear his crest, regardless of his status.”

 

Lena’s heart pounded in her chest. She felt the tears stinging her eyes and tried to look away. It was already hard enough to contain her emotions without Kara stepping forward and holding her by the shoulders. Lena didn’t want to look at Kara. She didn’t want her to see the tears, and the pain, and the shame she felt for being a Luthor.

 

But she could never refuse Kara.

 

She looked at Kara and saw nothing but love.

 

“Whoever marries you, Lena,” Kara said with the utmost conviction, “should be proud to be a Luthor. Because they’re taking your name.”

 

Lena bit down on her lip so hard it almost bled. She didn’t want to cry, damn it, she should be stronger than this. But no one had ever said something like this to Lena — it felt like some old wound on her heart had healed from Kara’s words.

 

She didn’t realise she had fallen forward until she was pressed against Kara’s chest and sobbing into her shirt. Lena cried and cried, not even sure why she was crying. For herself? For Kara, the little girl who lost her world and the woman who believed in a Luthor?

 

In the end it didn’t matter why Lena was crying. Kara held her in her arms all the same. Kara loved her all the same.

 

And if part of Lena wished that it had been Kara who was proud to be a Luthor, it would be ignored. This was enough. Kara’s love was enough.

 

It was more than enough.
