10. x.

Summary for the Chapter:

whoever commented abt supercorp and babies, you might like this one

Kara was cute. This was an indisputable fact. Lena would go so far as calling her downright adorable. But now Lena knew that there were levels to the cuteness of a Kryptonian.

 

By far, Kara holding a baby was a dangerous level of cuteness.

 

“Who’s the cutest widdle baby in the whole universe?” Kara cooed, “You are. Yes, you are.”

 

Lena smiled fondly as she watched Kara pepper kisses all over the baby’s face. The baby let out shrieks of laughter, clearly loving the attention and affection. Lena had never seen Kara with a child before and this was beyond her imagination.

 

Kara was currently talking to her through a video call. She was staying with the Kent family in Smallville, including the Lanes and the Danvers. Kara, of course, would never pass up an opportunity to spend time with her family. So Lena was left to her own devices in National City; this was the first time since Kara and her family arrived in Smallville that the two of them had called.

 

So Lena was sitting at her desk, making heart eyes at her phone instead of organising her workload.

 

The wait had been worth it, however. Kara’s gaze was as sweet and warm as honey as she stared down at her nephew: Jon Kent, the firstborn son of Clark Kent and Lois Lane.

 

Lena asked, “How old is he again?”

 

“Almost a year old now. They grow up so fast, man.” Kara said, a clear frown on her face.

 

She watched as Kara angled the camera down until Jon’s chubby face filled her screen. He looked at the camera with a curious gaze. Lena’s smile grew impossibly wider.

 

“Say hi to Auntie Lena,” Kara said, wiggling his arm back and forth in a wave.

 

Lena scoffed and hoped that, for once, Clark’s super-hearing failed him. She didn’t want him to get up in arms about Kara implying that a Luthor had any sort of relationship with his son. God, that would be a shitshow.

 

“He’s very cute,” Lena said, “but I don’t think I qualify as an aunt, Kara.”

 

Kara just ignored her. Jon let out a happy gurgle and she resumed placing kisses on his head. Lena watched them with a certain warmth blooming in her chest. Love. She had to remind herself that love wasn’t a taboo word. It was OK to feel love.

 

Kara gazed down at the boy and murmured, “He looks just like Kal did at his age.”

 

He really did. He had the blue eyes that all members of the House of El seemed to have, along with the black hair and rosy cheeks. The only piece of Lois that Lena could find in him was the button nose. But it wasn’t the baby in Kara’s arm that she was looking at: it was Kara who had her attention.

 

She had known Kara for three years now. She had seen her at her best and at her worst. Just by the slightest movement or pause, Lena knew when Kara was feeling off or pretending to be fine. In that moment, she could tell that the love in Kara’s voice was tinged with sorrow and yearning.

 

How many years had it been for Kara now since Krypton died? How often did she close her eyes and still see her family, living peacefully and in ignorance of what was to come? Lena could only imagine what Kara was feeling. Did she see Jon as a beloved nephew, or as a second chance that she never got with Kal?

 

She never voiced any of this. Lena would never break the happy spell that Kara was in. Instead, she hummed and said, “I don’t know. That button nose looks a lot like Lois’.”

 

Kara kissed him on his nose and said, “It makes him look so much cuter, though.”

 

“That we agree on.” Lena murmured.

 

The camera was moving again and the image on Lena’s screen was a mess. Kara looked down, saying, “Lena, watch this.”

 

She watched as Kara propped the camera up against the wall or something before backing away. Kara held out Jon away from her and Lena wondered what exactly she was supposed to be looking for.

 

And then Jon began to float.

 

Lena’s eyes nearly popped out of their sockets. The closer she got to her screen, the more she realised that she wasn’t hallucinating: the baby was definitely floating in mid-air and having a grand time doing so. Kara was standing by with her hands hovering below him, of course, ready to break his fall.

 

She quickly said, “He’s already got powers? But he’s only a baby!”

 

Her heart dropped into her lap as she watched Jon begin to fall. Effortlessly, Kara scooped him back into her arms and he let out another shriek of laughter. Kara only laughed when she noticed Lena’s anxious expression.

 

Kara walked towards the camera, saying, “Apparently Kal started manifesting his powers around the same age. He nearly broke Uncle Jon’s back during a tantrum once.”

 

For a moment, Lena wondered how one looked after a Kryptonian baby? Surely the superstrength must be an issue during feeding.

 

“Is Lois alright?” Lena asked.

 

“Yeah, he doesn’t have any superstrength or superspeed yet, thank Rao.” Kara said.

 

She brushed Jon’s hair back to how it was before and he reached up with his little hands to grab her wrist. Lena’s heart must’ve grown three sizes at the sight of it.

 

“ Fyrehb ! Did you check out my gifts?” Kara called out before murmuring, “I swear I put them on the pile.”

 

Lena watched as another woman came into view, holding a pot of food. If she had to guess based on what Kara called her, Lena assumed this woman was Martha Kent. She definitely looked the part of a farmer’s wife.

 

“Kara, all the gifts were lovely but the sweater ...” Martha’s voice trailed off.

 

Kara turned back to look at her, asking, “What’s wrong with it?”

 

“You can’t give him a pink sweater, dear,” Martha said, “he’s a boy!”

 

Lena snorted at the look on Kara’s face.

 

“I forgot this planet had gendered clothing. Saoviz ,” Kara murmured to Jon.

 

Lena laughed. In settings like this where she was surrounded by people who knew her real identity, Kara didn’t shy away from her alien heritage. From comparing Earth and Krypton to reminiscing about her childhood memories, Kara never held back. It was where Kara was most comfortable and it was Lena’s favourite version of her.

 

God, she missed Kara. Her office felt so much bigger without Kara in it and her couch was so empty. She missed when Kara would fly into her apartment just to hang out for a little bit. Had it really only been only a few days since she had left?

 

Lena hoped she sounded casual enough when she asked: “How long are you planning on staying, darling?”

 

Kara grinned up at her like she knew exactly what Lena was trying to hide. She said, “Two more days. His actual birthday is tomorrow morning and I’ve gotta lead his rights. I’m pretty much the only one qualified to do it.”

 

“Is this for a Kryptonian custom I’m not aware of?” Lena asked, a twinkle in her eyes.

 

While she had learned a good portion of the Kryptonian language, there were still parts of Kara’s culture where she was left in the dark. The academic in Lena was eager to eat up any piece of information that Kara was willing to part with.

 

Kara nodded, saying, “Yeah, it’s kinda like a baptism. Kolir Rao im , which means the touch of Rao. Just gotta hold him up to the sunlight, say a few prayers and offer blessings from each family member.”

 

It was Kara’s turn to feign nonchalance. And it was Lena’s turn to see right through it, smiling as she said, “You look very excited.”

 

Kara smiled bashfully at her. Jon’s little arms reached up and began tugging at the locks of hair he could reach.

 

“I really am. I haven’t done this since I was on Krypton. I never really got the chance to do it with Kal.”

 

Lena’s heart tore in two. How many milestones had Kara missed out on? How often did Kara wonder what would’ve happened if she hadn’t been knocked off course? Lena already knew that part of Kara blamed herself for not maneuvering the pod out of the way of the debris, despite the situation she was in.

 

Kara shook her head and smiled down at the baby, saying, “But I can do it for Jon and that’s enough.”

 

Hope, help and compassion for all . That was the tagline that CatCo had branded Supergirl with and Lena found herself agreeing: Kara certainly had all of those attributes, especially the hopeful part.

 

Before Lena could say anything, Kara’s head swiveled around and she was looking at something off-screen.

 

Frowning slightly, Kara said, “It’s Lena.”

 

Whoever Kara was speaking to said something else. Kara shrugged and then grabbed her phone that had been laying against the wall this whole time.

 

“Clark wants to talk to you,” Kara said, a conspiratorial smile on her face.

 

Lena’s heart began to speed up. She wasn’t scared of the man. This was Superman for crying out loud, the most valiant of humanity’s defenders. She had heard all the stories about the people of Metropolis sharing meals with him or how he didn’t mind stopping by for a chat once in a while. Of all the big name superheroes, Superman was the most pleasant.

 

But Lena’s relationship with Clark Kent was a different story. It had always been stained with tension and suspicion from both sides, and that had yet to change. Despite their shared love for Kara, the two of them tended to stay away from each other aside from polite conversation when necessary.

 

Since that fight two years ago, he was trying to mend his relationship with Kara and Lena knew that -- it was the only reason she remained civil with him.

 

So Lena swallowed her pride and waited. Kara passed the phone to Clark, who then walked outside onto the front porch. Clearly he wanted some privacy for this conversation.

 

Clark had a scruff going on. Even with those dorky glasses on, he looked like the farm boy he was at heart. His hair was tousled and his shoulders were akin to mountains. The saddest part about Kryptonians was that no matter how many near deaths and battles they experienced, they rarely had any scars to show for it.

 

No, they just carried in their hearts.

 

Clark cleared his throat, saying, “Lena.”

 

“Clark.” Lena said, matching his tone.

 

The tension had returned. Knowing that Kara was probably eavesdropping, Lena said, “Congratulations. The birthday boy looks happy and healthy.”

 

This earned her a genuine smile from the Man of Steel. For a moment, Lena saw a piece of Kara in him and she felt that familiar pang of fondness. The pride in Clark’s eyes were clear for any to see. Lena wasn’t talking to a superhero right now, but to a father.

 

“Thank you,” Clark said, “he’s more than I could’ve hoped for.”

 

Lena couldn’t stop herself from saying, “It’s interesting that human and Kryptonian DNA are so compatible. Although from what Kara’s told me, his Kryptonian side seems to be winning.”

 

Flight wasn’t exactly a normal milestone for human babies.

 

Clark laughed, saying, “Unfortunately, yes. I’d been hoping he'd be more human like Lois, if I’m being honest.”

 

Lena could’ve just laughed it off. She could’ve moved on and pushed the thoughts in her mind away. But all Lena could think about was all those times that Kara had cried because she missed Krypton. She thought about how excited Kara was to share everything Kryptonian with Jon because he was her nephew. She thought about the way Kara smiled at Jon, like he was the only good thing in the universe and like he was the pride and joy of the House of El.

 

And here Clark was, hoping that Jon came out more like Lois. Less alien. More human . More normal.

 

We aren’t the only ones who want to feel normal . Kara had said that to her once. But Kara’s normal was leagues different to Clark’s normal. Kara wanted to be normal because she lost everything that made her normal when Krypton died, and because she wanted a moment’s reprieve from her own trauma.

 

Clark wanted his son to be normal because he loved humanity, at times more than he loved his own heritage. Clark wanted his son to be human because that was what he considered normal -- he had only known humanity, unlike Kara.

 

Maybe Lena was being irrational. Maybe she was about to step out of line. But she was past the point of no-return now; Kara was too nice to say what needed to be said, but Lena wasn’t.

 

“Clark.” Lena said, the vitriol clear in her voice.

 

He looked down at the screen with a small frown. He could tell that her mood had shifted and he couldn’t seem to figure out why. That only made Lena more angry. How could he not understand what he said wrong? Did he truly understand nothing about Kara?

 

Kara and Clark might have been born on Krypton, but they were worlds apart.

 

Lena tried to keep her voice level as she snarled, “I understand you have your own grievances when it comes to your heritage and your family, but keep those thoughts to yourself around Kara. She doesn’t need to hear you say that.”

 

Don’t make Kara feel like being Kryptonian and the subsequent powers that come with it make her not normal . Don’t isolate her because you think being human and powerless is more normal than being Kryptonian. Don’t favour Jon’s human side more than his Kryptonian side.

 

Lena watched as so many emotions graced his face. Initially, Clark had frowned in confusion which quickly morphed into surprise. She swore there was a moment where he looked hurt but it quickly morphed into a muted rage.

 

He clenched and unclenched his jaw, before saying, “Good chat, Luthor.”

 

The ‘Luthor’ was a nice reminder, really. They might be civil most times but it always came back down to her surname, didn’t it?

 

They didn’t bother with goodbyes. Moments later, laughter and chatter could be heard once more and the phone was passed around. Lena schooled her features into a more relaxed expression, hoping Kara wouldn’t notice the rage simmering just under the surface.

 

Kara’s face filled the screen and Lena melted.

 

“So,” Kara drawled, “did y’all play nice?”

 

Y’all ? The last thing Lena needed was for Kara to pick up that stupid southern drawl from the Kent family.

 

Lena only smiled, saying, “Was there ever any doubt?”

 

Kara didn’t miss a beat and said, “Lots. Lots of doubt.”

 

Those doubts weren’t unfounded. Every time Lena and Clark took a step forward, they seemed to take two steps back almost immediately after. Most of the time it was because of Lena and her sharp tongue.

 

“You look tired, Lena,” Kara murmured.

 

Lena laughed a little breathlessly and sunk back into her chair. How long had she been in this office? When was the last time she had eaten something without Jess prompting her? The days seemed to crawl by without Kara, yet she hadn’t even noticed the sun setting behind her hours ago.

 

It was hard finding your centre when the person you’ve centred yourself around was gone, albeit temporarily.

 

Lena massaged her temples, saying, “The L-Cores are keeping me busy at work but you’re right, I should head home soon.”

 

Kara wagged a finger at her and said, “Don’t make me fly over there. You know I will.”

 

“Is that a threat or a promise?” Lena asked, a little more tenderly than she would’ve liked.

 

Kara seemed to melt at this. The two of them just looked at each other for entirely too long. Lena could feel an ache in her bones without Kara near her.

 

Quietly, Lena murmured, “Come home soon, Kara.”

 

Home. Did Kara know that she meant Lena’s apartment, not National City or Kara’s apartment or anywhere else in the world? Did she know that every moment that Kara was gone felt like a lifetime for Lena? For all of Kara’s powers, she was so unaware of the hold she had over Lena.

 

Lena hadn’t had a home in a long time. She was too young to remember her home in Ireland, and the Luthor manor couldn’t be called more than a prison. But when Kara wrapped her arms around Lena, that feeling of peace and contentment sure did feel like home.  

 

Kara smiled.

 

“I’ll be back before you know it.”



later .



“Do you have an estimate for the completion of this statue, Mr Caulfield?” Lena asked.

 

“It should be built within the month, ma’am,” the man said, “We’re so grateful for your support.”

 

Lena smiled and asked, “How could I not lend a hand? Supergirl has been a good friend of mine and an even greater protector of this city. A statue of her is the least I can do.”

 

It had been a week since Lena had seen the Kryptonian in question. Kara should be coming back to National City today so Lena wanted to wrap up this statue business before that happened. The last thing she needed was Kara and her super hearing to eavesdrop and ruin the surprise.

 

“Did someone say something about a statue?”

 

Oh, for fuck’s sake.

 

Lena whipped her head around to look behind her. Of course, she found Supergirl landing on her balcony. The woman was carrying a paper bag with the Big Belly Burgers’ logo and a tray of milkshakes in her arms. Lena swore she felt her heart soar at the sight of her.

 

The architect who Lena had been speaking with looked like he was on the verge of collapse at the sight of Kara. Lena was suddenly reminded that most people had never even seen Kara in person. Once you cuddle with a superhero, the wow factor tends to fade away.

 

“Oh my goodness, Supergirl!” He said, his voice sounding wobbly.

 

Lena pinched the bridge of her nose and closed her eyes, saying, “ Shisir . I wanted it to be a surprise.”

 

She heard Kara’s deep chuckle behind her. Kara placed the food and drinks onto Lena’s desk before leaning down and kissing the crown of Lena’s head. Lena just knew that this architect was going to tell everyone that he saw Supergirl kissing Lena Luthor.

 

Oh, CatCo was going to have a field day with this. More evidence emerges about a secret, forbidden romance between a Luthor and a Super ! Lena could feel the migraine coming on.

 

As Kara pulled away, she said, “You still have an accent when you say it.”

 

The fondness in Kara’s voice was impossible to miss. However, Lena chose to focus on the annoyance that bubbled up in her from being criticised for her accent.

 

“I’m working on it, darling.” Lena grumbled, “Not all of us can learn an entire language in an afternoon.”

 

Damn it, the pet name had slipped out. This was even more fuel for the fire this architect was going to start once he left. Lena couldn’t even be bothered to tell him not to: that would just make these rumours more credible, if it seemed like they wanted to keep it a secret.

 

Kara left her hand resting on Lena’s shoulder as she turned to look at the architect.

 

“Hello,” Kara said with a warm smile.

 

The architect nearly jumped out of his seat, saying, “It’s so nice to meet you, Supergirl. I wasn’t expecting to meet you today or I would’ve worn my good shirt.”

 

Lena snorted which earned her a squeeze on the shoulder as a reprimand.

 

Kara said, “It’s nice to meet you, too. Is this a bad time?”

 

Before Lena could even speak, the man said, “No, no! I believe Miss Luthor and I are quite done for today.”

 

“Are you sure? I could just wait here and--” Kara was cut off.

 

“No need,” he said, “It was lovely meeting both of you.”

 

He was gone before Lena could even process what just happened.

 

“Likewise,” Lena said into the empty air.

 

The two friends both turned to look at each other. The mischievous smiles on their faces matched perfectly. Kara let go of her shoulder and leaned against Lena’s desk, facing her now.

 

Lena could feel her whole body buzzing with happiness. She wanted nothing more than to surge forward and hug Kara as tightly as she could. But that would make her look desperate and Luthor’s weren’t allowed to look anything except smug or aloof.

 

“You were loving that,” Lena said, smirking at Kara.

 

Kara said, “Hey, I think I’m entitled to a bit of ego-stroking.”

 

Lena asked, “Is the statue not enough for you?”

 

At this, Kara couldn’t keep the beaming smile at bay. Her blue eyes seemed to twinkle as she said, “I can’t believe you’re actually doing that.”

 

“It was supposed to be a surprise but you just had to barge in here and eavesdrop.” Lena said, trying to look as serious as she could.

 

Kara didn’t take her seriously. With an exaggerated pout, Kara murmured, “I’m sorry.”

 

Lena scoffed and rose to her feet. They had had this conversation about Kara weaponising her cute face many times, yet Kara continued to do it.

 

“You’re evil, you know that?” Lena said, “Put that pout away.”

 

Kara’s face immediately broke out in a grin. Suddenly, Lena was pulled forward and pressed into Kara’s chest. She still smelled like toothpaste but she had a certain earthy scent that clung to her clothes this time. Probably from that farm that the Kent’s lived on.

 

More than anything, Lena was surrounded by warmth. Kara’s elevated body temperature, her passion for life, the intensity of her emotions -- everything about her screamed heat and warmth. And Lena was intoxicated with it as she melted into her embrace.

 

Home.

 

“I missed you,” Lena murmured.

 

Kara laughed, saying, “I missed you more.”

 

Lena rolled her eyes as she pulled away. That wasn’t likely but she wasn’t about to get into an argument with Kara about who loved who more. (Again.)

 

Instead, Lena grabbed the food and drinks, taking them to the couch at the opposite end of her office. Kara followed faithfully behind her. The couch had taunted her all week when Kara had been gone, reminding her of how many times the two of them had spent together on it.

 

Lena asked, “Did you have fun with your nephew?”

 

Kara made a strange noise and said, “He’s so cute, Lena, and he smells like milk. And you should hear it when he laughs. It’s like a burst of serotonin right to the brain.”

 

She made an explosive sound and Lena let out a small laugh. She had missed Kara’s silliness. It was a breath of fresh air amidst this bureaucratic hellhole she called a company.

 

Lena laid out the food onto the table, noting that Kara had bought her favourites from BBB’s: a veggie burger with garlic fries and a vanilla milkshake.

 

As she organised, Lena asked, “Got a bit of baby fever?”

 

Kara hummed, saying, “Only for this specific baby, I’m afraid.”

 

Lena wondered about that. Kara having children of her own, she meant. She wondered about it often since Jon had been born and Kara’s excitement had perfumed the air around her. She knew that Kara would be a wonderful mother -- the kind that Lena never had the chance to experience fully. But she also wondered what Kara, a Kryptonian in all possible ways, would be like with her own child.

 

What kind of rights and customs would Kara take part in? What parts of Krypton would she share? Would the child have Kara’s eyes? Lena hoped so, whoever the other parent was. Those signature blue El eyes were truly a sight to behold.

 

Sometimes, when Lena allowed herself a moment of weakness, she imagined what their child might look like. Lena hoped that the Kryptonian genes dominated like they seemed to in Jon; she wanted the child to have nothing from the Luthor’s. She wondered if the child would have black or blonde hair, or if they would be tall like Kara and Clark. But this was just a cruel fantasy that she indulged herself in. Nothing more.

 

Lena realised she hadn’t spoken for a while. Kara was watching her with a curious gaze from her seat on the couch.

 

Wearing her best smile, Lena said, “I, for one, am glad you won’t be having a child any time soon. You probably scared Lois half to death making Jon float around like that.”

 

“He’s gotta learn somehow! And I caught him every time he fell so it was fine,” Kara argued, a real pout beginning to form.

 

Lena noted that Kara didn’t deny that she had scared Lois.

 

She shook her head and said, “You’re incorrigible.”

 

Kara wore a grin, saying, “You love me.”

 

“Unfortunately,” Lena grumbled and fell back onto the couch.

 

Here, sitting with Kara on her office couch, eating familiar food and joking around is where Lena felt most at home. It was good to be back in Kara’s gaze.

 

“Meanie,” Kara said, “I might be bulletproof but my heart is delicate, you know?”

 

She placed her hand over her chest, feigning pain.

 

Lena rolled her eyes, saying, “You’re just a big baby.”

 

And so they fell into easy conversation once more. Lena talked to her about how L-Corp was doing now that her L-Cores (the solar power harnessing pods) were distributed globally and available for installation in everything from homes to stadiums. L-Corp was in an incredibly good position right now, which meant that Lena was working overtime to make sure everything ran smoothly.

 

Success never tasted so sweet. There were even talks about Lena being considered for a nobel peace prize, which was… baffling. She wasn’t sure what was more shocking: them considering a Luthor or them considering her .

 

The two of them talked about their families, their friends, their work. This was Kara’s graduation year before she began her veterinary school. Lena, of course, has already pulled some strings to make sure that whatever school Kara chose would have a vacancy specifically for her. The perks of being a billionaire were immeasurable.

 

They talked until all the food had been eaten and the drinks were gone. They talked until Lena forgot about whatever task she had been working on before then. They talked until Lena didn’t feel homesick anymore.

 

It was only until Lena’s lunch break alarm went off that Kara jumped to her feet.

 

“We should go out tonight,” Kara said, putting her hands on her hips.

 

Lena raised an eyebrow, asking, “Any reason why?”

 

“I’m just feeling great,” Kara said, “I’ve got an adorable nephew, National City is doing great and I finally get to see you again. Let’s just go out and have fun! When’s the last time we went on a date, huh?”

 

Yes, Lena’s heart skipped a beat at the word ‘date’. So what? She just hoped that Kara wasn’t paying attention to her heart beat like she usually did.

 

Lena said, “We went to that science conference just two weeks ago.”

 

“That was for work, it doesn’t count!” Kara argued.

 

Lena laughed and held her hands in surrender, saying, “Alright, alright. We’ll go out.”

 

Kara all-but leapt into the air and shouted, “Yes!”

 

She watched as Kara began to walk towards the balcony with a bounce in her step. Lena realised the woman was about to leave and jumped to her feet, saying, “Wait, you’ve got to tell me where we’re going first.”

 

Kara shot her a cheeky grin, saying, “It’s a surprise.”

 

“You just ruined my surprise for you, that’s not fair!” Lena said, crossing her arms in a huff.

 

“Dress casual. Luthor-casual.” Kara said, “I’ll pick you up at six on the dot!”

 

And with a two-finger salute paired with a grin, Kara soared off into the sky above them. Lena sighed as she watched her go. The sudden emptiness that settled in her heart was quickly replaced with elation when she realised she was going on a date with Kara tonight. Platonic, yes, but still quality-time with her favourite person.

 

Oh, no. What was she going to wear?



later.



As it turned out, Kara’s surprise had been a rather sophisticated date. She took Lena to an art museum where Kara explained small facts and tidbits about the artwork and artists. It was quite a beautiful museum, Lena had to admit. The artworks ranged from sculptures to paintings and it was certainly captivating to see.

 

It was also nice to have Kara guide her around with her hand resting on the small of Lena’s back the whole time.

 

She was glad that Kara told her to dress business-casual because she would’ve looked so out of place in a dress. Instead, she had worn a teal bodysuit under black slacks and heels. Kara had worn a button-up shirt, pleated pants and boots. Of course, Kara had driven them on her motorcycle so she wore Jeremiah’s leather jacket.

 

Part of Lena thought that this felt a lot like a real date. She chalked it up to wishful thinking or a result of indulging in her own fantasies too often.

 

When they had explored the entire museum, Kara took her across the city to a nice restaurant. And by nice, Lena meant that they needed a reservation a month in advance just to get in. It sat at the nineteenth floor of a skyscraper and they had to take an elevator to get to the entrance.

 

(Kara didn’t book a reservation months in advance, however. No, she simply had Jess tell the restaurant owners that this booking was for Lena Luthor and miraculously, a vacancy popped up. The Luthor villainy seemed to be rubbing off on Kara.

 

And Lena was going to have a long discussion with Jess about this when she went back to work tomorrow.)

 

For now, Lena had to stare at Kara’s dumb face as she stuffed dumplings into her mouth. Lena had learned long ago that she needed to claim the foods she wanted so that Kara didn’t immediately eat it. Like now, where Lena had eaten two of the dumplings while Kara inhaled the rest.

 

“Have I mentioned how much I love soup dumplings?” Kara asked, looking content.

 

Lena sighed, saying, “You have. Several times and with your mouth full. Chew your food before speaking, dear.”

 

It really killed the pseudo-romantic vibes that Lena had been getting all evening. Kara was lucky she was so damn cute.

 

Kara swallowed and grinned from ear to ear, saying, “But then how am I supposed to embarrass you in front of all these rich folk?”

 

“ Kuvaium ,” Lena grumbled but she was unable to stop the smile on her face. Kara stuck her tongue out at her.

 

Luthor’s were supposed to look perfect in public; poor table manners had been swiftly corrected and she began to resent those who were messy eaters. Yet the sight of Kara stuffing her face was more adorable than anything else.

 

God, she had it bad.

 

Kara spoke up, saying, “I still can’t believe you learned Kryptonian so fast, Lee.”

 

Lena looked up at her with a raised eyebrow and said, “I relearned Gaeilge in a month and learned Japanese even quicker. Give me some credit.”

 

“Yeah, I guess it’s just weird to hear a human speaking Kryptonian so well. Even Clark has trouble with certain grammar points,” Kara admitted, already reaching for another basket of dumplings.

 

Lena wasn’t sure who to thank that this restaurant didn’t serve potstickers; they’d never get to the main course if they did. Not willing to fight for a few dumplings, she just let Kara take the whole basket.

 

Just before Kara stuffed a dumpling into her mouth, she paused and asked, “Wait, relearned?”

 

Sighing, Lena placed her chopsticks down and said, “You know that I’m originally from Ireland. I must’ve had some understanding of the language before I travelled here to America with my father. So when I was a teenager, I just picked it back up again and banged my head against the wall until I was fluent. The sentence structure is quite similar to Kryptonian so it helped, I suppose.”

 

For the record, the help was minimal. Lena still had to perfect her grammar, the complex sentences, choosing honorifics, etc. It was a bit of a nightmare to learn this alien language but the look on Kara’s face whenever she spoke Kryptonian made it worth it.

 

Kara wore a dopey smile as she said, “You’re pretty incredible, you know?”

 

“Oh, I know.” Lena said with a smirk, “But I do like hearing it.”

 

The two share a conspiratorial look before digging back into their food. The restaurant was definitely worth the reservation: the food was simply exquisite and they had plenty of vegetarian options for Lean to choose from. 

 

Kara broke the short silence, asking, “Speaking of you being incredible, how’s L-Corp going? Those L-Cores must be raking in the big bucks.”

 

“It’s going much smoother than I anticipated. We’ve made profits in the millions and companies are scrambling over themselves to get a piece of the fortune. I can’t even remember how many solar companies have reached out to L-Corp in hopes of making a deal.” Lena said, a small frown forming on her face.

 

Kara wiggled her eyebrows and said, “Lemme know if any oil companies get in your way. I’ll rough ‘em up for you.”

 

Lena let out a small laugh at her. In truth, she did have a few alternative energy tycoons trying to sabotage the L-Cores at every point of its creation and distribution. She was sure that the only reason she wasn’t assassinated by now was because she was publicly friends with Supergirl and no one wanted to deal with that kind of enemy.

 

But the last thing this evening needed was Kara panicking over this news. So instead, Lena said, “I’m a big girl, I can handle it.”

 

Kara reached across the table with her free hand and held onto Lena’s hand. Even after all these years, a shock still ran up Lena’s arm at the touch. And Kara’s blinding smile didn’t help, either.

 

“But you don’t have to handle it alone if you’ve got me.” Kara said.

 

No, she absolutely wasn’t blushing.

 

“Yes, well--” Lena began to say but was cut off.

 

By what, you might ask? By the sound of a car being thrown onto the road down below on the city street. The smashing sound caused everyone in the restaurant to jump or scream, before many of them raced towards the window overlooking the city. Kara, too, turned around swiftly to see where the noise came from.

 

Letting go of Lena’s hand in the process.

 

The patrons of the restaurant were saying something about a large alien or a beast rampaging across the street below. Lena only believed them when she heard the deafening, bestial roar.

 

She sighed and began drumming her fingers against the table. There went their quiet evening out. Kara turned back to look at her sheepishly.

 

Lena raised an eyebrow at her, saying, “You gonna get that?”

 

As they spoke, a waiter approached their table with their meals. A vegetarian stew in a clay pot for Lena and too many dishes to count for Kara. Apparently the staff here weren’t too concerned about the monster currently rampaging near their establishment. Lena wondered if they just trusted Supergirl to take care of it.

 

The two of them sat there in silence as the food was set down. Kara was fidgeting with her glasses under Lena’s sharp gaze.

 

Once the waiter left, Kara mumbled, “I feel bad. This was supposed to be our night.”

 

There was another roar which sounded a lot like a bear trying to say ‘Supergirl’. Lena did her best to hold in a snort.

 

She shrugged and said, “You can’t help it. Tell you what: you get rid of that thing before I finish my meal and I’ll let you order the whole dessert menu.”

 

The wonder in Kara’s eyes was a sight to see. Breathlessly, she asked, “The crepes, too?”

 

“Mhm,” Lena said, nodding as she began eating her stew.

 

She didn’t need to look up to know that Kara had already taken off. That monster down there didn’t stand a chance; Lena made it personal. Nothing came between Kara and unlimited desserts. So Lena just ate her stew in silence while the rest of the patrons crowded towards the window, trying to get a piece of the action.

 

Some were recording the fight, others were talking amongst themselves. One brave soul actually recognised Lena and the next thing she knew, there was a man walking towards her.

 

He called out, “Hey, aren’t you Lena Luthor?”

 

Lena sighed and barely glanced at him. Oh, yes, please alert the whole restaurant that a Luthor was in the building.

 

“Where’s Supergirl?” He asked, looking every bit as frantic as he ought to be.

 

Sometimes Lena forgot that she was desensitised to all of this alien nonsense.

 

“Do I look like her keeper?” Lena asked, “Just give her a second.”

 

She paid him no mind. It was odd knowing that people knew she could just summon Supergirl, and that they were close. The novelty of Kara being Supergirl had worn off long ago; after Lena had done monthly repairs of her supersuit covered in suspicious ooze, superheroes stopped being dazzling.

 

Kara, on the other hand, was just as wonderful as always.

 

The stew had been delicious, really. It had been a few minutes since Kara had taken off and she was almost done eating. As Lena was about to take the last bite, she heard the sound of laminated paper wobbling. She looked up and found Kara sitting across from her, not a hair out of place.

 

“Cutting it close, Danvers,” Lena said before taking the last bite.

 

Kara grinned, saying, “Still made it. It’s dessert time.”

 

When Kara reached for the dessert menu, Lena smacked her hand away. Kara cradled her hand to her chest and pouted as if she wasn’t an invincible superhero.

 

“Ah, eat your dinner first,” Lena said before murmuring to herself, “My poor wallet.”

 

Kara was already starting her first meal when she said, “Your wallet could stand to lose a few pounds.”

 

Lena frowned, asking, “Is this the ‘eat the rich’ sentiment I’ve heard about online? Because I am only a begrudging member of the bourgeois.”

 

“No politics, only food,” Kara said, yet again through a mouthful of food.

 

What annoyed Lena the most was that Kara didn’t need to eat all of this food. She just needed to sunbathe for a little bit and she’d be right as rain, no sleep or sustenance required. Kara ate all of this because she liked the taste.

 

Lena was jealous, damn it.

 

Huffing, Lena said, “I hate that you can eat all of this and stay so fit.”

 

Kara blinked up at her and said, “I’m not in the best shape, to be honest. Alex says I’ve gone soft and she put me on this gym program.”

 

Then Alex was definitely going blind. Lena actually snorted when Kara told her this.

 

“But why? You’re absolutely ripped, Kara. Has she seen you? I mean, look at those abs! You just had three bowls of pasta and they’re still there. And don’t get me started on those arms-- ” Lena’s rant was cut off, unfortunately.

 

She could’ve gone on all night. She had practically memorised the shapes and contours of Kara’s body by now.

 

“Stop, stop, I get it,” Kara said, waving her hands at Lena.

 

Kara began to eat her food without looking at Lena who was smirking at her across the table. Ah, so Kara was embarrassed by compliments about her body. How cute. She really needed to send Kara those twitter threads about people admiring Supergirl’s body some time.

 

Which she only came across by accident and not because she actively followed the accounts dedicated to Supergirl and her attractiveness. Obviously.

 

“You’re blushing.”

 

“Don’t talk about it.”




later.




Kara dropped Lena off at her apartment after they left the restaurant together. Which meant that Lena was taking her least favourite form of transportation: a Kryptonian driving a motorcycle.

 

“When I ride on your motorcycle, I wish you had flown me instead.” Lena said, “And when you fly me, I wish you had driven me instead.”

 

It felt better to have solid ground under her shoes again. How Kara hadn’t received any speeding tickets yet was beyond Lena. She suspected that the DEO just overturned them somehow.

 

Kara parked the bike and pulled her helmet off. She was pouting at Lena but the latter was too busy staring at her messy blonde hair to concentrate. It should be illegal to look that good.

 

“I can’t win with you,” Kara whined.

 

Lena scoffed and said, “You just ordered hundreds of dollars worth of food, I’d call that a win.”

 

“Semantics.”

 

Kara made it a habit to walk Lena back to her apartment for as long as Lena could remember. The apartment complex that Lena lived in was far from dangerous; it was actually quite expensive due to how many security protocols were in place to protect the residents. But despite being perfectly safe, Kara still insisted on walking Lena home.

 

Maybe it was some misplaced chivalry or a sense of duty that Kara felt. Whatever it was, Lena didn’t stop her: it was nice to spend a little extra time with her, anyway.

 

Everything had been normal that night. The security had greeted the two of them when they entered the building, and no one was around as they made their way to Lena’s apartment. Having the penthouse to herself was a luxury that Lena loved, which she would freely admit.

 

It wasn’t until the two of them stood outside her front door that Lena realised something was off. She had one hand holding Lena behind her and when she looked back, Kara held a finger to her mouth to silence her. Kara faced the door and pulled her glasses down below her nose to scan around them.

 

“Stay here. If you’re in trouble, scream and I’ll come get you,” Kara murmured, looking at her with cold eyes.

 

“What’s going on?” Lena whispered, “Kara?”

 

Kara gave her a stern look, saying, “Stop talking.”

 

That was when Lena realised what was going on: Kara must have heard something inside Lena’s apartment. Or rather, someone. Kara never spoke so sternly to Lena.

 

Kara murmured to herself and her supersuit materialised over her clothes. Lena watched in silence as she inputted the code to Lena’s apartment. The moment the door beeped to signal the code was approved, Kara raced in so fast that she was only a blur to Lena. She waited with bated breath as she listened for something . There was a scuffle, a grunt of pain and then something being slammed into a wall. God, she hoped it wasn’t broken.

 

It was only until Kara called for her that Lena allowed herself to breathe again.

 

Rushing into the apartment, Lena was greeted to the sight of Kara pinning someone face-first against the wall of her living room. The intruder was a man dressed in all-black, bleeding from his nose and struggling against Kara’s iron grip.

 

Pooled around his feet were two objects that made Lena’s heart skip a beat. The first was her laptop that she used for everything, and the second was the Kryptonian database that Kara gifted her. Two very valuable items.

 

Lena looked up as the intruder grunted and struggled against Kara again. She sighed when she noticed the blood he was smearing everywhere.

 

“He’s getting blood on my walls,” Lena drawled.

 

It didn’t matter if he was an assassin, a thief or whatever else there was; the idiot should at least be clean in his practice.

 

Kara shoved his head forward and said, “You’ve taken something that doesn’t belong to you.”

 

He said nothing. Instead, he continued to struggle in Kara’s grip. Surely he knew that there was no way he was getting away, right? No human could escape the hold of a Kryptonian. Especially not one that looked as angry as Kara did.

 

“Start talking buddy, because I’m feeling awfully clumsy right now. And if I put some pressure right here…” Kara trailed off, pushing forward into him.

 

The intruder let out a cry of pain as she nearly popped his shoulder out of his socket. It wasn’t often that Lena saw Supergirl dealing with criminals in person but she knew that Kara wasn’t usually this aggressive. If this escalated, she might have to step in and save the idiot ruining her walls.

 

That idea quickly soured as the man snarled, “You alien scum will pay for what you’ve done to humanity. Especially you Supers.”

 

Lena let out a mirthless laugh, saying, “Ah, so you targeted this apartment specifically because I live here.”

 

“How far you’ve fallen, little Luthor. Sleeping with the enemy. How could you? After everything Superman did to your brother!” His voice was dripping with venom and his eyes seemed to burn with rage.

 

Lena was used to angry men -- it was a given in the corporate world, after all. She was also used to people claiming that she slept her way to the top or claiming she was sexually promiscuous. But what she couldn’t shrug off was this man proceeding to spit at her. Albeit, he missed, but the sentiment had been heard loud and clear.

 

And before she could even react, Kara slammed his head into the wall just hard enough to make him cry out again. Lena watched with dread as Kara’s eyes turned white from her heat vision being activated.

 

“Do that again and I will break every bone in your body, one by one.” Kara said, seething.

 

Lena stepped forward and placed her hand on Kara’s shoulder. She could feel the taut material of the suit and the tense muscles under it. Kara was shaking with rage at this point and in an instant, Lena knew that it wasn’t an empty threat.

 

“Easy,” Lena said, squeezing her shoulder.

 

Kara only relaxed by a fraction. At this point Lena worried that this idiot was going to talk his way into an early grave.

 

As if reading her mind, the intruder looked between Lena and Kara, saying, “Cadmus will bring you back into the light.”

 

Something clicked in Lena’s mind. In an instant, her mind went back to many months before and the strange business card that had been left on her desk.

 

We are Cadmus. Expect us.

 

“Who’s Cadmus?” Kara demanded.

 

For the first time tonight, he said nothing. He just continued to glare at Lena as if she was scum. A familiar gaze, no doubt.

 

“Take him back to the DEO. You won’t get anything else from him.” Lena said, “But we need to talk.”

 

Kara whipped her head around to look at Lena, clearly in surprise. The two of them just looked at each other for a while as a silent conversation played out between them. In the end, Kara huffed and peeled the man off of the wall. He struggled for a moment before Kara dragged him out onto the balcony and flew off with him.

 

Lena was left to stare at the blood smeared on the wall and wonder what exactly was going on. Who was Cadmus? That idiot was clearly a member and he had a grudge against Lena specifically. He was anti-alien, too, which made his disgust for Lena’s friendship with Supergirl obvious. Maybe Cadmus was just the name of some right-wing, anti-alien group that would disband after a few months like they usually did.

 

But in Lena’s gut she knew that wasn’t it. This guy might’ve been a grunt but he had managed to get past security into her apartment and he knew exactly what to look for. He didn’t grab anything expensive and he didn’t destroy her home. No, he wanted information. He wanted this alien device and Lena’s laptop filled with all of her important, encrypted files.

 

He wasn’t just a brave idiot that wanted to send a message: this was a planned attack. And it had Lex written all over it.

 

Lena hadn’t realised that she hadn’t moved since Kara left until she heard the woman land beside her. Suddenly, there were hands gripping Lena’s shoulders and she was looking into worried blue eyes. 

 

“Are you OK?” Kara asked, her eyes scanning over her body for injuries.

 

Lena nodded and said, “I’m fine. That wasn’t the first attempt on my life, after all.”

 

It took some time before Kara was satisfied that Lena was OK. Kara quickly pulled her in for a hug and Lena just knew that she was using her x-ray vision to scan the room for any bugs or cameras that might’ve been planted. She had always been such a worry-wart.

 

Lena could hear the venom in Kara’s voice when she said, “He spat at you.”

 

The two pulled away to look at each other. Kara was frowning in that way that made her scar stand out and Lena wished she didn’t love it as much as she did. It didn’t help that Kara was being so protective over her.

 

“Yes, and you dislocated his shoulder so I’d call it even,” Lena said, a warning lilt to her tone.

 

A dark look passed over Kara’s face.

 

“I should’ve done more.” Kara snarled.

 

Lena looked at Kara and realised with stark clarity where the line was drawn between Superman and Supergirl. They both believed in the good of humanity, yes, but Supergirl wasn’t above breaking a few bones while she delivered her justice. So when someone hurt one of the people she loved, she enjoyed hurting them in return.

 

She wasn’t sure how to feel about this. She was just glad that Kara defended her, at least.

 

The look on Kara’s face left after a moment, however, and she looked like a kicked puppy once more. She asked, “What did you wanna talk about?”

 

Ah. Right. A crime had been committed. Lena had forgotten about that for a moment.

 

“That name. Cadmus. I’ve heard it before.” Lena said.

 

Kara looked floored at this news so Lena continued, saying, “About a year ago, I got a card on my desk that said something about Cadmus and that I should expect them. I didn’t think anything of it at the time so I just threw the card out.”

 

“Why didn’t you tell me about this?” Kara asked, her voice raising just a bit.

 

Lena gave her a stern look and said, “Kara, if I told you about every ominous letter or death threat that I got, I’d never stop talking.”

 

Kara deflated at this. God, scolding Kara felt like scolding a pet. Lena gently slapped against Kara’s stomach because of the stupid pout that popped up on her face.

 

“Don’t give me those puppy-dog eyes. The threats have lessened since I met you.” Lena said, a little softer this time.

 

Kara looked at her with this odd expression. It was as if she was having an internal conflict and Lena could only watch it unfold in those blue eyes. What exactly Kara was conflicted about, Lena didn’t know. All she knew was one moment Kara was standing in front of her, the next she was zooming around Lena’s apartment.

 

In less than a minute, Kara was dressed in her pyjamas and the guest bedroom had been done up behind them. Lena’s heart raced when she realised what Kara had done -- and what she was going to do.

 

“I don’t want you sleeping here alone tonight. Or for the next few days. This guy wasn’t waiting here to kill you, he wanted to take valuable information from your apartment. This was a coordinated attack.” Kara said, her tone leaving no room for arguments.

 

While Lena knew that Kara was only voicing thoughts that Lena just had , she was still against this idea. For one, she didn’t want Kara worrying herself sick because of Lena. And two, she felt like Kara felt obligated to look after her tonight because of this attack.

 

Because after years of friendship, Lena still struggled to believe that Kara genuinely cared for her and enjoyed her company.

 

“I doubt there’ll be a round two tonight,” Lena argued.

 

Kara shook her head, saying, “Doesn’t matter, I’m not going to risk it. I’ll grab some stuff from home and sleep in your guest room for a few days.”

 

Lena knew she’d never talk Kara out of this. That woman was as stubborn as an ox, especially when it came to Lena. And it wasn’t like Lena really wanted to push Kara away. They hadn’t had a sleepover in so long since Kara had been in Smallville for that week.

 

A rush of fatigue went through Lena’s body as she processed everything that had happened. God, she needed some sleep. She sighed and rubbed at her temples, murmuring, “You worry about me too much.”

 

Kara stepped forward and pulled Lena into her chest. Lena pressed her head into Kara’s chest and simply melted into her. She had missed this feeling of being engulfed by Kara. When she felt Kara rest her chin on top of Lena’s bowed head, she wondered if the Kryptonian woman had grown. When had she gotten this big?

 

With a kiss pressed on top of Lena’s head, Kara said, “You don’t worry enough about yourself.”

 

Lena didn’t argue. She just wanted to be held for a little longer. Thoughts of Cadmus and work were miles away from her mind right now.

 

Kara was back home. Lena could handle anything now.

11. xi.

Notes for the Chapter:

bit late with this update but oh well :D enjoy some supercorp, losers <3

Lena was sprawled out on her office couch, heels kicked off and head thrown back. She kept her eyes closed because they felt so heavy after a long day of staring at stupid men and screens. What she wouldn’t give for a long dip in the bath once she got home tonight.

 

Most of L-Corp’s workers had returned home by now. Only a few stragglers, Lena included, still remained. She had long finished all her duties and organised herself for the following day, but there was something else she needed to attend to.

 

Her most lucrative hobby: supersuit development.

 

Supergirl landed on her balcony with only a gentle flap of her cape to announce her arrival. Lena hummed when she heard this sound and pried her eyes open. Kara wore her most brilliant smile and it sent Lena’s heart racing in her chest.

 

Instead of acknowledging this, Lena checked her phone and said, “You’re late.”

 

Kara raised an eyebrow, saying, “Excuse you, I flew all the way to Columbia to get you this coffee.”

 

At the mention of coffee, Lena’s eyes flew open and she jumped to her feet. Kara held the cup out to her and the logo printed on it was unfamiliar to her. Greedily, Lena grabbed the cup as if it were some precious treasure to be hoarded.

 

Kara looked entirely too proud of herself as she smirked down at Lena and crossed those muscular arms over her chest. God, whatever workout program Alex had her on was doing wonders for her.

 

Lena rolled her eyes and said, “You can fly at the speed of light, darling. No excuses.”

 

Kara gave a small huff in response. Lena took a generous sip of the coffee and the warm ichor dripped down her throat like honey. The favour was so clean that Lena felt like she had been cheated by drinking the swill they serve here.

“God, this is good,” Lena murmured before licking at her lips.

 

Kara pressed her lips against Lena’s temple, saying,  “Only the best for you, the nicest friend in the world who’s building me a super-cool new suit.”

 

The supersuit in question was now pressed against Lena’s body. She found herself reaching out and placing her hand on Kara’s stomach, trailing her hand down as she felt the material. It was surreal to know that there were millions of nanobots under her touch that were so tightly woven into each other, they felt like one solid piece. And that’s saying something, because she made the damn thing.

 

How many battles had Kara been in while she wore this suit? How many blows had she taken? How many close calls? This suit had been through it all, a symbol of hope to humanity and a symbol of their friendship to them.

 

Still, her gift wasn’t able to protect Kara when she needed it most: those anti-kryptonite shields had failed and the memory of that night was tucked away into a tiny box, encircled with chains and locks.

 

“I’m considering building a new version of this suit, honestly. I’m most concerned about those anti-kryptonite shields that I built. Clearly those didn’t work and it was a disaster. But that might take some time,” Lena trailed off, her mind racing with ideas.

 

Kara stilled underneath her touch. She leaned back and looked down at Lena, who looked up at her with a small frown. She already knew what was coming.

 

“Lena,” she murmured, “You know that… thing with the red kryptonite, it wasn’t your fault.”

 

That thing being Kara rampaging through National City, fracturing her relationship with Lena and Alex, and nearly killing several people. Not to mention traumatising Kara herself when the kryptonite had been pumped out of her system. Kara made it sound so minimal, so insignificant, when she referred to it as “that thing”.

 

Lena smiled mirthless and asked,  “Wasn’t it? I built the shields and I didn’t even bother to check them against actual kryptonite. No, it was my own hubris.”

 

Kara’s eyes were impossibly blue as they gazed down at her. She sounded so sad as she murmured, “Lena.”

 

Lena’s hand still rested against Kara’s stomach. Underneath her suit, she could feel flesh and blood and warmth. If Lena continued to be careless when it came to ensuring Kara’s safety, that might not be the case one day. No, she would never allow herself to be so careless again.

 

“Please, don’t argue with me on this.” Lena said, pulling away from Kara with a sigh.

 

The sudden space between them was felt by Lena in her very core. She felt like she had been pulled away from the warmth of a bonfire and thrown into an icy ocean. But she needed to feel like this -- part of her didn’t feel like she deserved to be in Kara’s embrace anyway.

 

There was a moment of silence. Then, Kara said, “I won’t push it. But that doesn’t mean I agree with what you said.”

 

Lena turned to look back at her. Kara met her gaze, unwavering in her resolve. Ever the valiant defender of Lena Luthor, even to the woman in question. Lena wished she didn’t find it as admirable as she did.

 

Softly, Lena said, “Thank you.”

 

Kara smiled at her, saying, “For what it’s worth, though, everything else about the suit works perfectly. You should give yourself more credit.”
