The two of them strolled around the room. As they passed by the guests, Lena shook some hands and nodded wherever necessary to keep up appearances. Some of these people were friends of the family (not necessarily friends of Lena Luthor, however) while others were colleagues and business partners. In Lena’s eyes, they were nothing more than fattened pigs drooling at the sight of a feast: the Luthor name. Fame, prestige, wealth — her family name had it all, as of recently. And each one of these people wanted a piece of it.

 

Kara trailed beside her dutifully, smiling at all the right people and looking oh-so handsome as she did so. Lena had been wondering what Kara’s thoughts were regarding tonight’s guests and when she finally asked her, the response had been interesting.

 

“They look at you like a piece of cake,” Kara said, a fake smile plastered on her face as she looked around, “and they’re all starving. It reminds me of the Science Guild, really.”

 

Lena hid her smile behind a wine glass. Perhaps Kryptonians and humans weren’t so different after all — Supers and Luthors even less so.

 

Eventually, the two of them settled by the railing that overlooked the entire ballroom. The bar was directly below them and the guests were stretched out for Lena to gaze down on, like some divine being. Was this what Kara felt when she flew over National City? One could develop a god complex if they drowned themselves in this feeling.

 

Lena ignored that strange thought, asking, “Did you park your motorcycle properly this time? The pavement outside L-Corp is not where it belongs.”

 

“If it’s not then why do the security guards let me park there?” Kara asked, wearing a mischievous smile.

 

Lena grumbled, “Because you charmed them with your weird alien magic.”

 

Kara puffed out her chest and asked, “Is that your way of saying I’m cute?”

 

To cut an internal rant short, Lena was sure that if Kara did have some alien magic that allowed her to enthral people to her whims, Lena was certainly under her spell.

 

“No,” Lena deadpanned.

 

She turned to gaze down at her guests while Kara laughed. She worried that if she looked at Kara in that suit any longer, she would do something she would regret immediately after. Would Kara let her do something stupid? With all of Kara’s new flirty behaviour, would a kiss be her line in the sand? Lena hated uncertainty and it seemed like this line of thinking was filled with nothing but uncertainty.

 

Lena had to remind herself that flirting and actual romance were two different things. Kara was straight, damn it, she was just having some fun.

 

Kara’s voice cut through her thoughts, saying, “You’re doing it again.”

 

Lena snapped her head to the side.

 

“Doing what?” Lena asked, feeling her heart rate speeding up.

 

Kara nudged her chin and said, “Judging people.”

 

Relief flooded through Lena. Ah, Kara was just referring to Lena looking down at the guests. Lena rolled her eyes, saying, “I prefer the term people watching.”

 

She watched as Kara leaned against the railing with both arms, holding a glass filled with scotch and coke. The alcohol would do nothing for Kara but she apparently just liked the taste. Lena wasn’t sure if she liked the taste of the liquor or the coke, really.

 

Kara tilted her head and she watched as blonde locks spilled down across her shoulder. Lena’s heart squeezed painfully.

 

“Alright, who are we judging?” Kara asked, grinning from ear to ear.

 

Lena’s gaze slid away from Kara to the guests below. Inhaling sharply, she said, “Well. There’s the Harlows over there. A perfectly normal family, if you ignore the numerous scandals that seem to crop up every other day. The current heir is proving to be a less than favourable candidate to inherit the company because of his gambling problem so the sons and daughters of the current CEO are tripping over themselves to prove their worth.”

 

Kara hummed thoughtfully as she gazed at the couple Lena had pointed out.

 

She continued to scan the room until she settled on her next target. Lena said, “See that woman by the bar in the fur coat? Delilah Greenwood. She’s well known for dating men young enough to be her grandsons.”

 

“That’s…” Kara trailed off and her eyebrows were raised to her hairline.

 

Lena giggled, saying, “Oh, don’t misunderstand. She’s delightful company. We go to the same spa retreats in the summer.”

 

Kara scoffed at her. Lena’s gaze returned to Kara and the two were quiet for a moment, appraising the other. Eventually, Kara had broken the silence.

 

“Sometimes I forget you’re a member of high society.” Kara said, “You probably know everyone here by name, and which family they belong to. It’s… impressive but kinda scary.”

 

Lena shrugged, saying, “It’s easy if you’re born into this life. I grew up in a viper’s nest and quickly learnt that no one was my friend, only adversaries to judge and compare myself to. The more homework you do about the people who run in the same circles as you, the less likely you are to be blindsided.”

 

She was far too young for that to happen anymore. But the lessons she had learned had never been forgotten, and she carried the memories of betrayal and deception like a badge of honour. Lena’s circle of trust was small and she intended to keep it that way. Whoever said to keep your enemies close was an arrogant fool.

 

Kara’s hand was warm against Lena’s and the fingers that curled into hers were even moreso.

 

“I see why you hate these parties.” Kara said.

 

Truthfully, Lena would rather be at home with Kara right now. They could watch the rest of that medical show Kara got her roped into or they could do their own things in comfortable silence, interrupted only by soft words and each other. Lena wouldn’t mind being down in the labs tonight, either. Anywhere except near the stench of these so-called elites. And Kara seemed to recognise the disgust Lena felt. Ever the bloody empath.

 

“Sometimes they can be fun. For example,” Lena trailed off, looking around the room.

 

She found her target almost instantly. She leaned close to Kara and murmured, “You see that couple over there? Down by the balcony doors. They’re the Windsors. They used to scramble to insert themselves into my life once. I couldn’t get rid of them when they cornered me at parties.”

 

Kara nodded, asking, “Is there a reason they’re ignoring you now?”

 

“They come from old money.” Lena said with a smirk, “Oil money, specifically.”

 

Kara let out a snort in surprise. “Ah. You messed up the family business.”

 

Lena let out a tortured sigh, saying, “Yes, I suppose I’m the villain of this tale. The big, bad Luthor provided clean energy to the world so the uber-wealthy of America has to make-do with a few measly millions to their name. How ever will they cope?”

 

“For a millionaire, you really hate rich people,” Kara mused.

 

Lena grimaced and said, “As I said, I’m a begrudging member of the bourgeoisie. I’d rather have millions and do actual good with them, rather than donate it all to charity so I can claim some false sense of moral high ground.”

 

It grew quiet between them. Lena watched Kara who was still staring down at the Windsors. Kara’s jaw was clenched and her grip on Lena’s hand grew just the slightest bit tighter. No matter how angry Kara ever got, she always handled Lena’s body with the utmost care and it was something that Lena never failed to notice.

 

There was a deep rumbling in Kara’s chest, similar to a growl. Lena wouldn’t have been able to hear it if it wasn’t for their proximity. Kara murmured, “I don’t like what they’re saying about you.”

 

Figures they would badmouth the host of the party they were attending. Money couldn’t teach manners, after all.

 

Lena hummed, asking, “And do you plan on throwing them into the sun?”

 

“I might.” Kara said, no trace of humour in her voice.

 

Lena sighed before cupping Kara’s cheek and turning her face. Finally, she had made Kara look at her and not the Windsors. No one else , a more possessive part of Lena had thought.

 

“Let them talk. It’s all they can do now. Their business can no longer exploit the world and its people in the face of such fierce competition, so they’re throwing a fit like any ill-mannered child would.” Lena said, her voice dropping an octave as she spoke.

 

Kara still looked ready to fling those two in the sun but Lena’s words and touch seemed to distract her. From the corner of her eye, Lena spotted another nuisance quickly approaching them.

 

“And speaking of a viper’s nest…” Lena murmured before (unfortunately) taking her hand away from Kara’s cheek and wearing her most approachable smile.

 

“Lena!”

 

The man approaching them was the mayor of National City, appointed recently and with Lena’s support. He was the only viable democrat candidate. Lena still found his personality to be rather lacking.

 

Lena said, “Mayor Blackwood. Lovely to see you tonight.”

 

The two of them shook hands. Lena was acutely aware of Kara’s grip on her waist reappearing now that someone had invaded their little bubble. Parties like this always put Kara on edge just because of that one time someone tried to assassinate Lena at a gala. It was sweet, if a bit much.

 

Blackwood grinned, saying, “Great party, I must say. You haven’t thrown one of these in a while.”

 

“Work keeps me busy.” Lena said, adding, “I’m sure you can sympathise.”

 

Make these idiots feel important and they’ll be eating out of your hands. That’s one of the few lessons Lena’s father ever taught her. It never failed.

 

“Indeed. And who’s your friend here?” Blackwood asked, wiggling his eyebrows on the word “friend”.

 

Kara pulled Lena impossibly closer, before giving the mayor a tight smile. Was Kara slouching before or did she suddenly grow a few inches? Surely she wasn’t posturing in front of the mayor of all people?

 

“Kara Danvers. Pleased to meet you.”

 

Kara stuck her hand out and gave Blackwood a firm handshake. As they shook, Blackwood squinted at her and asked, “Has anyone ever told you that you look just like Supergirl?”

 

At this, Kara raised an eyebrow and said, “Maybe I am her.”

 

Lena's heart leapt into her throat while Blackwood let out a hearty laugh.

 

“I like this one,” Blackwood said, “You two enjoy your night.”

 

Ignoring her racing heart, Lena said, “And you, Blackwood.”

 

It was only until the mayor had disappeared from Lena’s sight that she allowed herself to breathe again. She let out a huff before turning to Kara, grumbling, “I have no idea how you get away with that. Those glasses hide nothing! Your scar is still there, how do they not notice?”

 

Kara shrugged and said, “Most of them have never seen Supergirl up close, let alone my scar.”

 

“And they don’t know you got it from whacking your head on a table.” Lena said with a small grin.

 

Kara whined, “Rao, the lecture jeju gave me about running around the house after that.”

 

“And your ukr ?” Lena asked.

 

Kara smiled brightly at the sound of Lena speaking in Kryptonian.

 

“He wouldn’t stop laughing.”



shortly after.



The two friends remained in their little bubble for some time after that. They talked and argued and did whatever they could to pass the time until their actual target appeared. And thankfully, they didn’t need to wait too long. Lena glanced below them at one point and her eyes automatically settled on the newly arriving guests: five grim looking men, all dressed in black and sporting several scars.

 

Bingo.

 

Lena nudged Kara and hissed, “Kara, stop eating the shrimp, they’re here.”

 

With a mouthful of seafood, Kara said, “On it.”

 

Kara rushed off somewhere to get out of sight to change into her supersuit. Lena had plenty of time to get down the stairs and finish her speech, thanks to Kara’s superspeed. This knowledge didn’t stop her heart from racing, however. Those men were likely armed and she had no idea what they were looking for tonight. Were they looking for a fight? Money? It was anyone’s guess.

 

For now, Lena had her part to play and she intended to play it well.

 

The guests clapped for her as she stood on the stage. There was no microphone for her to use so she would just have to use her voice. Shame.

 

“Thank you all for coming tonight. As you all know, we are here today to celebrate the success of L-Corp,” Lena began, pausing to allow the audience to finish their applause. It felt good, alright?

 

Lena continued, saying, “I know most of you remember when this company had a different name and very different leadership. But we have come a long way since then. Thanks to Supergirl’s support, we have been able to develop clean, efficient solar energy storage. Supergirl is a dear friend of mine, despite our families’ history. And I, for one, want to live in a world where a Luthor and a Super can work together for the good of humanity.”

 

She eyed the goon squad and could tell that they really didn’t like how chummy she was with Supergirl. Good. Their anger would be used against them.

 

“With that in mind, please welcome National City’s very own protector: Supergirl!” Lena called, before gesturing to the empty space beside her.

 

In the blink of an eye, Supergirl had swooped down and stood beside Lena. The audience was ecstatic at this point and Lena could feel her own stomach erupt with butterflies at the sight of Supergirl grinning down at her. It was only until she felt a pair of lips pressing against her cheek that she remembered: oh, right, this is my Kara.

 

Kara pulled away and turned to look at the crowd. With that famous smile, Kara called out, “Thank you, thank you! Please, enjoy the food, I know I will.”

 

The audience laughed but it sounded muffled and distant to Lena. She was too busy ignoring the blood rushing to her cheeks and focusing on the goon squad. Despite Kara’s visible affection to Lena, they weren’t reacting beyond their permanently grimacing faces.

 

Lena frowned.

 

As Kara pulled them away, Lena murmured, “Is Winn ready?”

 

As subtle as possible, Kara looked away from the guests surrounding them and down at her own feet. After a moment, Kara murmured back: “Yup. He looks super comfy under the stage. Are those potstickers in his hand?”

 

Lena rolled her eyes before saying, “Dance with me, you glutton.”

 

Kara’s gaze snapped up to settle on Lena who was pulling her towards the middle of the room. Lena waved at the orchestra (yes, a bloody orchestra, the DEO made her go all-out) and they began to play a slower song. Perfect for partnered dancing.

 

“What? Why?” Kara asked, suddenly sounding less like Supergirl and more like Kara Danvers.

 

Lena stopped walking and turned around. The two of them were facing each other now, and Kara was frowning down at her. Despite her confusion, Kara wasted no time in placing her hand on Lena’s waist and placing the other hand in Lena’s.

 

“Because we’re supposed to be luring them out.” Lena said, “If they’re as anti-alien as I think they are, they’re going to hate this.”

 

Before Kara could argue, the dance began and off they went. One of the best parts about dancing with a Kryptonian was that they knew your next move before you did. When Lena leaned right, Kara already knew exactly which way to pull her and how much strength was needed. Just by watching Lena’s movements, Kara was able to perfectly execute ballroom moves that took others far longer to learn. Kara’s kinaesthetic intelligence was truly a sight to behold and a joy to experience as Lena’s dance partner.

 

As they continued to dance along the ballroom, Lena asked, “Did you get taller?”

 

Kara raised an eyebrow at her and asked back: “You do know that Kryptonians don’t stop growing until we’re twenty-five, right?”

 

“You’re kidding me.” Lena murmured, feeling a little awestruck at the thought.

 

Kara grinned, saying, “Nope. Still got three more years till then. I might be as tall as Clark one day.”

 

Lena scoffed as Kara twirled her, saying, “I hope not. You’re already a menace at a humble five-eight.”

 

Kara only laughed in response. The two continued in their dance, bodies pressed flush against each other. Lena was drowning in the smell of Kara’s shampoo and the feeling of her hands on Lena’s body. Lena knew that their photos were being taken and a discreetly-recorded video of Supergirl and Lena Luthor dancing would be headline news tomorrow. For now, Lena didn’t care. She was happy and she wanted to enjoy this moment for as long as she could.

 

She only noticed the necklace Kara wore over her supersuit until the light glinted off of it. It was a pale blue gemstone carved in the shape of a teardrop, encased in a silver metal. Her eyes just couldn’t look away.

 

Unable to stop herself, Lena asked, “That necklace is beautiful. Where did you get it from?”

 

Kara smiled gently at this.

 

“Krypton.”

 

Lena stumbled over Kara’s foot in surprise and as she fell, she knew that this fall was going to hurt like a bitch against that marble floor. Thankfully, Kara caught her in mid-air until she was bent over Lena and the necklace she wore rested against Lena’s chest. Kara’s hair was pulled into a ponytail so Lena’s eyes greedily drank in the sight of Kara’s face hanging directly above hers. She was faintly aware of the sound of cameras clicking, capturing the sight of Supergirl holding Lena as if this was all part of the dance.

 

Still bent over Lena, Kara murmured, “It was my mother’s necklace.”

 

Lena could only hold her breath as Kara pulled her back to her feet. Now, with the two of the standing face to face, Lena allowed herself to breathe again. With Kara’s gentle touch, the dance continued and Lena’s dignity remained intact.

 

“I’ve never seen it before.” Lena said, still eyeing the necklace.

 

To think that such a simple piece of jewellery could hold so much weight to it: the all-consuming weight of a mother’s love was stored inside of it, after all. And judging by the fond look on Kara’s face, this necklace was probably her most prized possession in the whole universe. 

 

Kara said, “I don’t usually wear it in case it gets damaged or lost.”

 

“So why tonight?” Lena asked, gazing up at her.

 

The expression on Kara’s face was unreadable for once. Kara led her in the dance silently for a while, before saying, “Just felt like it, I guess.”

 

There wasn’t much substance to Kara’s words but they made Lena’s heart race nonetheless. When had Kara’s hand slid across her back until her forearm was pulling Lena into Kara’s front? When had their faces gotten so close? Had Kara always looked so beautiful in her suit?

 

“I stand by what I said,” Lena said, her voice thick with emotion, “It’s beautiful.”

 

Kara smiled, saying, “It’s a family heirloom. This gem was carved from the Jewel Mountains thousands of years ago.”

 

Lena couldn’t even imagine how old the necklace actually was. Although they blended in well enough, both Kara and this necklace belonged to a world that humans like Lena could never reach and yet… here Kara was. Holding her close. Here . A miracle, truly. It was only more proof for quantum entanglement, really.

 

Licking at her lips, Lena whispered, “It matches your eyes, you know?”

 

There was an unfamiliar look in Kara’s eyes at this. It was something intense and heavy and the memory of it would sit with Lena for weeks after. The intensity of Kara’s stare was overwhelming and Lena wanted to drown in it.

 

But it was gone before Lena could consider it. Kara’s gaze turned to someplace behind Lena and a sombre look formed on her face. Ah, the goon squad must be moving. What a shame. Kara pulled away and Lena tried to ignore that sinking feeling in her gut to no avail.

 

“Duty calls.” Kara said, giving Lena’s hand one final squeeze.

 

Lena nodded and said, “I’ll get to Winn.”

 

The two of them separated and the chaos began. Supergirl made quick work of the goons before they could even draw their weapons. Alien tech — the DEO’s intel had been right. Winn and Lena had worked together to deactivate the weapons preemptively, while Supergirl carried their unconscious bodies back to the DEO.

 

The guests of tonight’s party weren’t as upset at the terrorist presence as much as they were upset about the party being cancelled. Truly, Lena will never understand these people and perhaps that was a blessing in itself.

 

Fucking rich people.



later.



Lena liked laboratories. She liked being in them, she liked the convenience of them, she liked working in them. What Lena didn’t like was abandoned Cadmus labs that looked like a set of a horror movie. And she was going to voice her displeasure no matter the company, damn it!

 

“I understand that you found one of the Cadmus bases,” Lena grumbled as she stepped over some tubing, “but why am I here?”

 

Somewhere in the distance the sound of generators working could be heard, as well as some strange noises Lena didn’t care to decipher. The place gave Lena the creeps.

 

Kara grunted as she chucked some debris to the side. She turned to look back at Lena, saying, “The agents we interrogated said that they were working for Lex Luthor. Maybe something of his is here and you could identify it.”

 

Ah, so that’s why Kara had wandered onto her balcony in her supersuit, begging her to come with her. In standard Kara fashion, she refused to tell Lena where they were going.

 

(“It’s a surprise, Lena.”

 

“It’s always a surprise with you! How about I surprise you with how far I shove my foot up your—“

 

“Woah, there! No threats of bodily harm! I’m a sensitive woman, y’know.”

 

“Yeah, well, I’m sensitive to being dragged around by a— Kara, put me down!”)

 

The two of them walked further into the laboratory. No matter how close she walked to Kara, she couldn’t shake the feeling of being watched and the general negative energy that filled the rooms.

 

Lena murmured, “I’m not sure this is safe.”

 

Kara laughed and said, “I’m right here, you big scaredy cat.”

 

Huffing, Lena grumbled back, “Well, not all of us are bulletproof.”

 

There's weird shit everywhere. Lena had seen a bunch of stolen alien weapons, some weird goop or blood or ooze (ew) on the floors and tables, evidence of a struggle, large vats of unknown chemicals, etc. Whatever Cadmus was hoping to investigate or more likely dissect in here was beyond Lena. Whatever happened to being clean in one’s practice? This mess was unacceptable for scientists to work in, let alone cause.

 

Kara broke the silence, saying, “Rao, this place is creepy. Reminds me of the dissection labs from school.”

 

Lena paused.

 

“Midvale or Krypton?” Lena asked.

 

Kara snorted and said, “Both, really.”

 

They continued to walk. The longer they walked, the more Lena saw and the less she understood. There was nothing in here that was familiar. God knows why the DEO thought otherwise. And Lena couldn’t even concentrate on what she was seeing because a more pressing matter had her attention: synthetic kryptonite. Her conversation with Alex only a few days ago had been weighing heavily on her.

 

She had a few nights to sleep on the idea of working with kryptonite. Deep down, she knew what she wanted to do: she wanted to protect Kara, first and foremost. And if handling and studying kryptonite meant that she could create the shields that would protect her, Lena would do it. But that didn’t mean she was happy to do so, nor that she was ready for whatever reaction Kara had to her decision.

 

Kara, and likely Clark, were unpredictable when it came to the subject of kryptonite. It was like they were different people. Lena had no idea what Kara’s reaction would be and she was dreading it.

 

But she can’t put off this conversation any longer. Although the location wasn’t ideal, she had to say something now or she’d never find the courage to do so later.

 

Steeling herself, Lena stopped walking and took a big breath. Kara noticed this immediately and turned back to look at her. Now or never.

 

“There’s something I need to tell you,” Lena said, looking up at her.

 

Kara frowned a bit, saying, “Sounds serious.”

 

“It is.” Lena said with a sigh, “I just want to start by saying that I wasn’t initially interested in this offer. I was against it at first.”

 

“OK.” Kara murmured, drawing the word out.

 

Lena asked, “So you know about the mole in the DEO, right?”

 

“Yeah, they took some of the kryptonite that DEO had.” Kara said, adding, “I can’t believe they were manufacturing kryptonite and didn’t tell me.”

 

Lena frowned, asking, “Didn’t Alex know?”

 

Kara shook her head and said, “She had no idea. There are some things that only Hank knows as the director.”

 

So Alex had been telling the truth. Lena knew that she had been, but her suspicious nature forced her to check anyway. Lena scrunched up her face, saying, “Right. Well, the DEO made me an offer.”

 

Silence. It settled between them, heavy and thick. Lena could feel the blood rushing to her face as she waited for Kara to say something. Anything.

 

Kara crossed her arms over her chest and asked, “What does this have to do with the mole?”

 

Lena took a deep breath. Finally, she said, “They offered me some of their remaining kryptonite.” 

 

“And you accepted?” Kara asked, the distress evident on her face.

 

Lena hated the way she asked that. That accusatory tone, as if this was Lena’s idea, that she was actively seeking out kryptonite. Lena glared at Kara, saying, “It’s not like I want it for some nefarious reason. I’ve been trying to improve your shields but I can only do so much when I’ve never even looked at kryptonite before.”

 

“So you want kryptonite for me?” Kara said with a mirthless laugh.

 

The rage was boiling in her belly now, burning her up from the inside and threatening to burst out. Lena didn’t deserve this. She didn’t deserve this attitude from Kara, especially not from Kara of all people.

 

“Yes. The DEO wants me to study kryptonite and make working anti-kryptonite shields because I’m the best woman for the job.” Lena said, the rage she felt spilling out in her voice.

 

Kara met her heated gaze before shaking her head, saying, “I don’t like this.”

 

“What is the problem?” Lena asked, exasperated, “Do you not trust me with it?”

 

“I don’t trust anyone with it! Lena, that thing almost killed me once!” Kara said, the fear and anger all blending together.

 

Lena raised her voice to match Kara’s, snarling, “And I would never let you anywhere near it! I’ll be working with this stuff at the DEO base where it’s safe. What, do you think I’m just gonna throw you some of it and say ‘catch’? Give me some credit.”

 

Kara threw her hands up and said, “It’s not about that!”

 

“Then what is it about?” Lena asked, her voice thick with emotion, “Because right now it feels like you’re angry with me for no reason.”

 

Kara stepped forward. Lena could tell that this conversation was shoving a wedge between them but there was no stopping it now. This relationship would never last if they didn’t have this conversation, if these feelings didn’t come out. It would fill up their lungs and suffocate them from the inside. No, Lena needed Kara to tell her the truth. Was it Lena or the kryptonite that scared her?

 

Shaking now, Kara snarled out: “It’s kryptonite , Lena. It’s the only thing that can kill me — kill Clark and Jon!”

 

Lena swallowed down the lump that had formed in her throat. She stepped forward. Kara leaned back but didn’t move.

 

“You could kill me.”

 

Kara’s eyes grew wide. Lena took another step.

 

“You could kill me in the blink of the eye. You almost did once. I still remember that day, in my apartment. I know you do.” Lena said, the words spilling out of her mouth like poison.

 

Red eyes, corrupted veins, she’s going to kill me, oh God I’m going to die—

 

“Lena,” Kara whispered, looking so damn hurt, “that was the kryptonite.”

 

Lena nodded, saying, “I know. That’s what I’m trying to protect you from. Because I know that wasn’t you that day. That’s why I still let you touch me. Hug me. Because I trust you.”

 

It was too much. This tension between the two of them, this silence that roared in Lena’s ears — it was too much. Lena stepped forward until the two of them stood in front of each other. Kara refused to look at her, looking down at her own shoes.

 

Softly, Kara said, “It’s not that simple.”

 

Lena knew how much Kara hated kryptonite. She knew how much pain it caused, almost irreversibly so. But she also knew that this was a bitter pill that they both had to swallow or, one day, kryptonite would ruin everything.

 

She reached out and slid her hands into Kara’s. On instinct, Kara’s hands curled into hers and held on. Lena squeezed her hands, murmuring, “It is. Just trust me. That’s all I’m asking from you.”

 

For a moment, Kara continued to stare at the floor. Then, she glanced up at Lena and she could see the pain in her eyes. Lena’s heart clenched at the sight.

 

“What if—” Kara began to ask but was cut off.

 

The sound of gas hissing from a room down from them had startled both of them. Kara had pushed Lena behind her, shielding her with her own body before Lena could even react. Lena’s heart was in her throat at this point.

 

Peeking out from behind Kara, she asked, “What was that?”

 

Kara peered down the hallway, likely using her x-ray vision. After a moment, she murmured, “It’s… a huge vat.”

 

“Anyone there?” Lena asked, gripping onto Kara’s arm. Never had she been more grateful for Kara’s invulnerability than in that moment.

 

“No,” Kara said, “but there’s something there.”

 

With that, Kara began walking further in. Lena followed closely behind her, the previous conversation feeling like a distant memory now.

 

It didn’t take them long to find the source of the noise. They walked into a room that looked like the stuff of nightmares: a dimly-lit room with a large tank filled with unknown liquids and pumps attached to it situated in the centre of the room. The glass of the tank was too foggy to see anything inside and there's a panel behind them. Very weird. And, of course, nothing was familiar to Lena.

 

Kara walked towards the tank and Lena asked, “Can you see what’s in it?”

 

She nodded at Lena before turning back to the tank. Lena watched with bated breath. After a moment, Kara gasped and leapt backwards, slapping a hand over her mouth.

 

Kara spoke as if the wind had been knocked out of her, rasping out: “There’s no way.”

 

Lena raced over and grabbed Kara’s arm, asking, “What? What did you see?”

 

Once more, Kara pulled Lena behind her.

 

“Can you get that panel working, Lena?” Kara asked, adding, “I need to stay between you and this tank.”

 

Lena had never seen Kara look this shaken. Whatever was in that tank had scared Kara down to her bones, and that scared Lena. In a daze, Lena nodded before racing to get to the panel. It wasn’t difficult to operate it: there was still some power leftover and Lena had multiple PhDs. After playing around with it, Lena skimmed through all the files it had stored. She settled on the most recent entry, which had a dossier and a voice memo attached to it. The moment she activated it, a hologram popped up of a scientist she didn’t recognise.

 

The hologram began to speak, saying, “Clone 217, the first promising specimen. The previous clones had plenty of defects and issues so they have been… disposed of. Regarding this specimen, everything appears to be working as intended. Cell structure is identical to the desired parent and nil abnormalities exist as of this recording.”

 

The hologram disappeared. Lena frowned and mumbled, “A clone. But whose clone?”

 

Kara seemed to be frozen in place as she stood in front of the tank. Whatever Kara was seeing with her enhanced vision was really getting to her. Lena needed answers. She continued to dig through the files. There’s so much to go through and not much of it is helpful. In the end, Lena found what she was looking for in the first entry ever stored here.

 

Nothing could have prepared her for the answer she got.

 

Lena braced herself onto the panel, breathing out, “Oh my God.”

 

Kara whipped her head around to look at her. Lena could feel her knees turning to jelly and the room spinning around her.

 

“What is it, Lena?” Kara asked, a tinge of panic colouring her voice.

 

She stared at the tank. Inside that tank and litres of green fluid…

 

“It’s a hybrid.” Lena murmured.

 

Kara asked, “Of who?”

 

“Superman.” Lena said, “And my brother.”
